Abstract (英文): Deep-research agents repeatedly decide what to search, read, and verify, often using large cloud-hosted language models for action proposal. However, privacy and local control motivate running agents on users' devices, where limited memory and compute favor smaller models and complicate on-device training. Yet inference-time guidance faces three obstacles: exploring alternatives through real execution adds tool calls, prompted self-evaluation is bounded by the agent's judgment, and model-specific guidance risks repeated adaptation costs. We introduce Seer, an inference-time framework providing local guidance that separates the agent's action proposal from learned consequence prediction and value estimation. From a shared corpus of real research trajectories, we train two models: a tool-response simulator on observed call--response pairs and a value estimator on histories labeled with discounted terminal correctness. The simulator and estimator are trained offline and reused across agents without further training. Moreover, Seer executes only the selected action and adds only real tool responses to the committed history, avoiding tool calls on discarded candidates at additional inference cost. Across multiple deep-research benchmarks, Seer substantially improves agent performance for different language models. For instance, GAIA accuracy rises from 13.6% to 41.7% for Qwen3.5-4B and from 22.3% to 50.5% for Qwen3.5-9B. Furthermore, our analyses suggest that broader candidate coverage can compensate for imperfect simulation, while prediction and scoring errors limit the benefits of wider imagined search. These findings highlight the potential of reusable models for prediction and evaluation to strengthen small research agents through extra local computation without the real execution of the discarded candidates.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pretrained language models (LMs) remain unreliable on exact multi-digit arithmetic without gradient fine-tuning or external tool interpreters. We introduce closed-form, zero-gradient circuit-grafting primitives that compile deterministic computation directly into pretrained LM weights using zero training examples and zero runtime hooks, while bounding per-layer normalization drift and preserving natural-language (NL) competence. Our construction combines three analytical mechanisms: (1) two-layer Mellin-GLU quadrature over bias-free SwiGLU/GeGLU layers, turning unknown per-token RMSNorm scaling into translation along a log-spaced gate grid ($>250\times$ dynamic range) with IEEE 754 error bounds; (2) cross-token scale cancellation via signed Chebyshev Indefinite-RoPE self-sink attention and memoryless SiLU-antisymmetric GatedDeltaNet shifts; and (3) axis-aligned width extension ($d \to d+K$) with $\sqrt{d/(d+K)}$ norm compensation, Base-1000 canonical BPE emission, and differential $(+254,-256)$ tied unembeddings. An automated 5-stage compiler synthesizes 4-8 digit signed $\{+,-,\times\}$ circuits in constant $\mathsf{TC}^0$ depth alongside 3-literal Boolean (100%, $N=1{,}728$; plus 42-layer Gemma 4 E4B audits) and 5-bit Majority/Parity (100%) circuits. On 6-digit arithmetic ($N=1{,}200$, BF16), IBM Granite 4.2 3B (1.07M-1.33M active non-zeros, $\le 0.036\%$) and Alibaba Qwen 3.5 4B (2.25M-2.39M non-zeros, $\le 0.057\%$) reach 99.17% and 100.00% ALU-prefix accuracy (100% with an FP32 sidecar) and 98%-100% mid-sentence resumption, outperforming 12.8K-sample LoRA (42.2% / 61.7%, 0% inline continuation). A factorized block-sparse sidecar holds A100 memory at 7.89 / 10.65 GiB (4.60 / 8.14 GiB under INT8/INT4 + FP32 sidecar) while passing all 14/14 pre-registered $>2$K-token Tango splits ($N=4{,}392$; 0/11,134 OOD triggers).

Abstract (英文): Set pieces produce about a third of non-penalty expected goals (xG), yet they are usually evaluated by what happened next. We present Monte Carlo Pass Search (MCPS) for set pieces, which treats a corner or a lofted free kick as a distribution over plausible executions and plausible futures instead of a single realised event. From the pre-kick state, MCPS proposes counterfactual deliveries and counterfactual set-ups (where the attackers stand at the kick), plays each out many times in a world model (3D ball physics, a generative model of all 22 players, the keeper's come-or-stay decision and a Player-to-Touch model) and scores every future with a value model. On 249 deliveries from 30 Serie A 2025/26 matches, with every model applied only to matches it never saw, the best available delivery is worth 0.0085 xG more than the one kicked (+28 %), mainly because it reaches an attacker before the keeper. Searching where the attackers stand as well adds +0.0027 xG with the observed ball and +0.0033 on top of the best ball; designing their runs too adds +0.0007 more. We show how to keep such a search honest, and estimate how much more data each component needs before a club could rely on it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Real-time task guidance is inherently flexible: when an agent guides a user through a task, multiple responses and timings can be appropriate, and sometimes the best action is to remain silent. Existing benchmarks often miss this flexibility by treating synthesized dialogues as fixed targets for what to say and when to say it. We introduce FLUID (Flexible, Evidence-Grounded Situated Task Guidance), a unified framework for modeling, evaluating, and enabling flexible interaction in multimodal task guidance. Using naturally occurring human-human guidance from WTaG and HoloAssist, FLUID grounds interventions in their supporting evidence, necessity, and timing flexibility, and uses this representation both to evaluate flexible guidance and to build a real-time agent for task guidance. Our agent achieves the strongest overall balance of response correctness, intervention precision, timely guidance, and response latency among evaluated systems, while still leaving room for improvement in avoiding speech overlap. Together, FLUID moves situated task guidance beyond rigid reference matching toward more natural and adaptive human-AI interaction. Project page: https://fluid-guidance.github.io.

</details>

Yet reasoning can substantially improve decision quality, raising the question of whether its benefits can be retained without the cost of generating it. We investigate this question with \textbf{GLiDE}, a family of decision models spanning 0.4B to 27B parameters.
  Building on Qwen hybrid language models, GLiDE enables bidirectional interactions between the context, question, and candidate answers by removing causal masking from full-attention layers. To transfer reasoning capabilities into single-pass inference, we combine soft-target distillation from multiple teachers, including a reasoning model, with progressive post-training on diverse decision tasks. We further explore parameter-free recurrent depth to increase inference-time computation without generating tokens.
  Across five model sizes, GLiDE lies on the performance--parameter Pareto frontier of the Hugging Face \DI{}, with each model outperforming evaluated alternatives of equal or smaller size. \dmtwo{} (27B) achieves a hidden-set score of 71.52, with an expected calibration error of 2.0\% and a median latency of 92.9\,ms on a single GPU. Further checkpoint averaging and parameter-free recurrent depth yield GLiDE~27B, which reaches 73.37 with an expected calibration error of 2.1\%.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Intersectional bias arises when a model treats people who hold several marginalised identities at once differently from what their separate identities would predict. Auditing such intersectional subgroups remains a challenge: their number grows quadratically, and there is no agreed baseline for what independent attributes would predict. Without such a baseline, many existing audits flag a subgroup when it exceeds each attribute alone, a condition that attributes acting independently already satisfy. We test this standard criterion on 150 synthetic datasets built with no interaction. With the shuffle null, it flags 66.1 false subgroups per dataset on average, and at least one in every dataset. When we plant interactions of known size into these datasets, a repaired null lets the criterion recover only 32% of them. We introduce ADDIS (Additive Intersectional Screen), which fits an additive model over all attributes, scores each subgroup by its Additive Residual Distance (ARD) from that model's prediction, and tests it against a Freedman-Lane null. On our synthetic benchmark, ADDIS flags 0.09 false subgroups per dataset and recovers 84% of the same planted interactions. We then build IndiPersona, 10,000 synthetic Indian personas with six identity attributes, and audit 13 open language models. The standard criterion flags 7.1% of the 738 tested subgroups on average; ADDIS flags 3.0%. Gender identity crossed with sexual orientation is the largest pairing in every model, and counterfactual personas confirm it and additionally reveal a caste x class interaction in all but one model that the observational test lacked the data to detect. Much of what audits report as intersectional bias can thus arise from independent single-attribute effects alone, yet a small core of interactions survives.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Linear probes are the cheapest LLM lie detectors but often fail to transfer across kinds of lie. Label-free directions from honest and deceptive role-play are untested at 70B scale and on lies about knowledge, where a lie and an honest denial can be identical. We widen a published persona bank with roles that deny knowledge they have, plus honest counterparts. At each model's middle layer the resulting persona direction ranks lies across Liars' Bench better than single-dataset probes on three of its four models; it fails on Qwen, even when Qwen stays in role far more often. On Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct it beats directions from a random persona bank, but its gain over the published direction is within the range of random widenings. On lies about knowledge, persona directions mainly track a reply's wording: where lies and honest denials share their words, our three main contrasts stay inside the random-direction band on Llama and Qwen and reach only its edge on Gemma, though on Mistral one clears it. A knowledge direction contrasts questions the model can answer with ones nobody can, each followed by the same scripted denial. It separates lies from identical honest denials on two models and narrowly on a third, but not on the fourth; its other results partly depend on the benchmark's wording. On the Gender-Secret fine-tune it clears the band by a few hundredths on Llama and fails on Mistral. The sum of the two directions ranks lies better than a supervised probe at the same depth on all four models (on one, both near chance) but is level with the persona direction on Llama's average and behind that probe on held-out lies. More varied role-play questions do not help the persona direction on Liars' Bench.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When multimodal decision models determine actions, moderation outcomes, or quality judgments, their reliability depends on preserving semantic decisions across presentation changes, ignoring irrelevant additions, and distinguishing information consistent with task evidence from information that conflicts with it. Existing evaluations study decision-interface biases and multimodal model behavior in separate settings, leaving no common protocol to compare multimodal decision models with their backbones on practical tasks. We introduce \textbf{MM-SystemOneBias}, which evaluates 13 models on 5,323 original instances from nine public datasets and 11 task scenarios using six paired interventions: option order, option label, external judgment, image--text description, irrelevant information, and rating scale. Across seven matched decision-model--backbone pairs, decision models are more stable under option-label, irrelevant-information, and rating-scale changes in all seven pairs; six pairs also show lower option-order flip rates. They incur smaller accuracy losses under incorrect external judgments in all seven pairs and conflicting descriptions in six. In six pairs, they gain less accuracy from correct external judgments and show less favorable accuracy changes under consistent descriptions. Among pairs with unchanged semantic decisions, mean absolute changes in answer probabilities range from 4.56 to 11.55 percentage points, revealing input effects that accuracy alone misses.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Graphic designs are ordered compositions of RGBA layers whose separation enables selection, movement, and reuse. Existing layered models represent each layer with a full-canvas or region-sized spatial latent grid, so representation size grows with the cumulative spatial extent of the layers. We present ProtoDiffusion, a latent-diffusion framework built on ProtoVAE, a multi-layer variational autoencoder that encodes a design into one shared spatial prototype and one candidate vector per layer. Each candidate conditions a shared decoder to reconstruct its corresponding RGBA layer. For $N$ layers, the representation contains $HW+N$ tokens before backbone-specific packing, compared with $NHW$ for full-grid-per-layer representations. ProtoDiffusion jointly denoises the prototype and a fixed set of candidate slots, conditioned on an image or text, with bounding boxes as optional layout guidance. The model leaves unneeded slots empty and determines the number of layers and, in absence of boxes, their layout, requiring neither as input. The same architecture supports both image-to-design and text-to-design. ProtoVAE achieves high-fidelity reconstruction from its compact shared latent representation, reaching 40.43 dB PSNR on layer crops. On the LayerD evaluation split, image-to-design achieves RGB L1 scores of 0.040 with bounding boxes and 0.057 without, with box-guided alpha soft IoU (0.850) comparable to the strongest fine-tuned baseline (0.871). On merged images, text-to-design reaches 66.41 FID versus 86.84 for FLUX.2 [klein], achieves a 0.860 text-accuracy score, and runs $19.1\times$ faster than the same-backbone generation-then-decomposition cascade.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-language models (VLMs) integrate visual and textual information, but their outputs do not reveal internal concept representations. Global workspace theory (GWT) proposes that information is selected from parallel brain processes, integrated in a shared workspace, and made available to specialised processes. We introduce the Multimodal Concept Lens (MC-Lens), a multimodal Jacobian lens, to study these functions in VLM concept processing. Its vision encoder and language decoder lenses map intermediate states to a shared semantic space defined by the decoder's output vocabulary to compare concept accessibility across modalities. MC-Lens supports internal concept interventions to examine the role of accessed concepts in downstream computation. (i) Accessibility and selective access. Multimodal concept information is readable in intermediate states and selectively accessible as tasks require. We extend "readout carriers" from one name token to a token set to better capture and express concept information and support counterfactual experiments. (ii) Concepts in downstream computation. Counterfactual experiments show that accessed concept information participates in downstream computation and reasoning. (iii) Development of cross-modal alignment and selective access. We develop two J-space metrics tracking the development of cross-modal concept alignment more clearly than hidden state cosine in controlled VLM training. The experiment also shows that reliable selective access develops later than concept accessibility. (iv) Shared functions across models. Across VLM families and sizes, we find shared patterns of concept accessibility, query-dependent selectivity and cross-task intervention effects despite differences in internal organisation. These findings offer an experimental basis to study GWT in multimodal artificial systems and how it can guide cross-modal alignment improvements.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent AI agents can generate playable games, but these games are not necessarily engaging. Mastery is a key source of engagement, yet designing for it requires extensive human play and tuning. We propose PlayCraft, an automated framework that uses a vision-based agent to play games and draws on its detailed play experience to revise them for mastery. Starting from an initial game, PlayCraft alternates between playing and crafting. In the playing stage, a player agent watches the game screen, uses the controls, and pursues the game objective over repeated episodes while retaining memory. After each episode, it reports its progress, outcomes, decisions, and reflections. In the crafting stage, a crafter agent uses these reports to revise the game. It estimates player abilities, predicts performance at different proficiency levels, and compares those predictions with a target learning progression. It diagnoses the game and addresses the gaps by giving players meaningful opportunities to improve through better decisions and execution. We evaluate games in fresh runs with player agents across three intelligence settings, measuring learning gain and learning slope. Compared with the initial games, PlayCraft yields larger learning gains and steeper learning slopes in most games. It also outperforms the baselines on the macro-average of each metric at every intelligence setting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban villages (UVs) are informal settlements embedded within Chinese cities, and their boundary delineation is fundamental to urban renewal and refined spatial management. However, accurate segmentation of UVs relies on both visual characteristics and the spatial organization of buildings, yet previous studies have often relied on simplified representations of building morphology that inadequately capture spatial configuration. We propose DBEFNet, a dual-branch encoding and fusion framework that exploits remote sensing imagery (RSI) and building morphology heatmaps for fine-grained UVs segmentation. The RSI branch combines CNN and Swin Transformer encoders with multi-scale feature extraction to capture and integrate local appearance and broader spatial context. In the Building branch, multiple building attributes are transformed into morphology heatmaps and adaptively weighted to characterize building-cluster patterns. A hybrid attention fusion module (HAFM) models bidirectional dependencies between visual semantics and building morphology. Experiments across 15 cities yielded an overall accuracy (OA) of 94.79 % and a mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) of 86.24 %. Ablation experiments indicate that incorporating building morphology improves mIoU by 0.87 % over the enhanced RSI-only model, while adding the HAFM provides further gains of 0.93 %. Compared with the RSI-only model, DBEFNet achieved higher mIoU in 11 of the 15 cities, with an average city-wise gain of 1.92 %. Trained on Beijing, DBEFNet achieved 75.79 % mean mIoU across 12 unseen target cities, demonstrating its cross-city generalization capability. These results underscore the critical role of building morphology and the value of integrating morphological cues with visual appearance for precise UVs mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D object detection in construction-scene LiDAR sensing remains challenging because sparse returns, irregular layouts, heavy occlusion, and diverse machinery make object representation and localization difficult. Dense manual annotation in such scenes is also inefficient and costly, creating a strong need for annotation-efficient learning. Existing active-learning methods often struggle in this setting because they tend to favor uncertain but redundant samples while overlooking representative scene coverage. To address this problem, we propose Hierarchical Clustering-enhanced Active Learning (HCAL), an active-learning framework for LiDAR-based 3D object detection in construction and roadwork environments. HCAL improves sample selection under limited labeling budgets by identifying informative candidate keyframes, suppressing redundant uncertain samples, and preserving representative coverage through entropy filtering, gradient-space clustering, and density-consistency selection. To support field-relevant evaluation, we further establish ConVeh3D as a construction-scene LiDAR benchmark for annotation-efficient 3D detection and unknown-category-aware evaluation in a controlled category-withheld setting. Experiments on ConVeh3D, with KITTI as secondary supporting evidence, show that HCAL achieves the best average detection accuracy across most tested labeling budgets. The results indicate that HCAL provides an effective framework for improving sample efficiency in construction-scene LiDAR-based 3D object detection under limited labeling budgets. The dataset and code will be made available at https://github.com/zxk688/ConVeh3D .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coastal wetlands are characterized by diverse vegetation, high spectral similarity, fragmented spatial distributions, and dynamic environmental conditions, posing significant challenges for accurate hyperspectral image classification. Although UAV hyperspectral imagery provides fine-grained spatial and spectral observations that can help capture such complex and fragmented features, effectively leveraging this information to distinguish spectrally similar and spatially intermixed vegetation types remains an open problem. Therefore, we propose a Progressive Spectral Gated Residual Network (PSGRNet). Specifically, a spectral enhancement and multi-scale sequence modeling strategy is first introduced to strengthen discriminative spectral representations and capture multi-scale spectral dependencies. Building on these enhanced representations, a frequency-aware module further exploits global frequency-domain characteristics to aggregate informative spectral features adaptively. To effectively integrate both spectral and frequency-domain representations, a GRF strategy is developed to improve the robustness of the network in heterogeneous and fragmented coastal wetland scenes. We have collected three UAV-based hyperspectral datasets covering representative coastal wetland scenarios, including reed-dominated natural wetlands (NT-YK), highly fragmented landscapes with dynamic land-cover transitions (YC-YS), and human-influenced estuarine environments (LYG-LH). Experimental results on these three datasets demonstrate that PSGRNet outperforms 9 representative methods, achieving macro F1 scores of 89.25%, 81.99%, and 81.29%, respectively. These results verify the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed method for accurate coastal wetland classification and demonstrate the value of the released datasets. The datasets will be publicly available at https://github.com/HAPPYFST/Wetland-HSI-Data .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rural residential land in metropolitan suburbs often expands, stabilizes and contracts within the same region, making static zoning inadequate for anticipatory governance. Existing land-use prediction approaches commonly use attribute vectors or remotely sensed image features, limiting their ability to reduce input dimensionality while preserving the spatial structure and combined effects of locational, accessibility and planning-related drivers. This study converts abstract geospatial drivers into red–green–blue (RGB) images and integrates them with convolutional neural network (CNN) models and ensemble prediction. Using suburban Beijing as a case, we evaluated 78 three-driver configurations across five deep-learning architectures, yielding 390 models. The modified VGG16 model achieved a weighted F1 score of 0.749. The optimal configuration combined village size, distance to the city center and expressway accessibility, indicating that rural residential land change is mainly associated with local development inertia, metropolitan gradients and external connectivity. A model using all nine drivers as nine-band input achieved a weighted F1 score of 0.758, compared with 0.749 for the three-driver RGB model; the latter used substantially fewer drivers while retaining higher precision and clearer variable semantics. Under the 2035 planning scenario, 36.57% of valid grids were predicted to increase, 33.90% to remain unchanged, 0.26% to decrease, and 29.27% were identified as uncertainty areas. These areas require flexible planning, dynamic monitoring and the avoidance of premature rigid decisions. By linking Earth Observation-derived land-change data with spatially structured driver representations, the workflow supports the explanation and prediction of land-use transitions and more adaptive governance of metropolitan suburbs.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tracking the ecological quality of many cities over many years needs a Remote Sensing Ecological Index (RSEI) that stays comparable across space and time, yet standard constructions do not deliver one. A single national principal component analysis (PCA) is dominated by the between-city climate gradient, which loads heat and wetness with the wrong sign and inverts the index. We build a de-gradient national PCA (de-gradient PCA) whose weights come from the within-city component covariance under a fixed global normalization. The resulting index recovers the expected component signs where the single national PCA inverts them. It agrees with MODIS net primary productivity, an external ecological reference (city-mean r = 0.59 ), even after controlling for greenness (partial r = 0.50 ), and its weights are stable to city resampling and to leaving out any representative year. Applied to all 125 prefecture-level resource-based cities of China over 2003–2023, mean RSEI rose from 0.61 to 0.65 ( p < 1 0 − 4 ), with 83% of cities on a rising trend, faster than in 245 non-resource cities. Resource type set the ecological level more than development stage, and 13 cities in the arid northwest and coal belt stayed near 0.30. A spatial Durbin model identifies built-up land as the strongest local pressure and industrial structure as a persistent negative regional spillover; random-forest importance corroborates the built-up ranking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mowing frequency is a primary indicator of grassland management intensity, forage productivity, and biogeochemical dynamics. Yet, despite a growing body of literature, operational frameworks capable of mapping cutting regimes at a regional scale remain computationally demanding and lack independent benchmarking. Here, we evaluate the potential of Google AlphaEarth annual satellite embeddings to map grassland mowing frequency across Denmark (2017–2023) at 10-m resolution. Using farmer logs across multiple years (n = 346), Random Forest emerged as the top-performing classifier, achieving an overall accuracy of 0.73 and a macro F1-score of 0.65. Our workflow substantially outperformed the operational Copernicus Land Monitoring Service (CLMS) Grassland Mowing Events (GRAME) benchmark (overall accuracy of 0.24). Latent embedding dimensions strongly correlated with macro-climatic and vegetation drivers (|ρ| > 0.8). Once deployed across the full nation extent, under 6 min of computing time, the model predictions proved ecologically consistent across pedological and agronomic gradients. We further applied Dirichlet regression to quantify how hydrological drought, indexed by a 6-month scale Standardised Precipitation-Evapotranspiration Index (SPEI-6), affects national mowing patterns. The extreme 2018 drought triggered a landscape-wide management de-intensification, shifting the intensive (≥4 cuts) regime into a 2 cuts system. During moderate droughts, regional responses diverged sharply: western regions exhibited agronomic resilience, whereas drier eastern islands repeatedly reverted to lower-intensity management. These results demonstrate that satellite foundation models can underpin scalable, automated agronomic monitoring and reveal grassland management’s acute sensitivity to drought, insights essential for adaptive land-use planning and national-scale environmental reporting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The imperative for sustainable agriculture has led to the promotion of conservation tillage practices, which are critical for maintaining soil health and reducing negative environmental impacts. However, the ability to verify the adoption of these practices at a regional scale and with minimal costs remains a challenge, particularly in regions with diverse crop systems. This study presents a satellite-imagery-based random forest classifier that integrates crop class information and a customized importance weighting technique for field-level, regional-scale mapping of tillage practices in crop-diverse regions. We use eastern Washington State, U.S., with multiple grains, legumes, and canola as a case study. Using 577 field-level ground-truth data points across two counties, the tillage classifier achieved a median overall accuracy of 84%, representing a 15-percentage-point improvement over the baseline median overall accuracy of 69%, driven by the inclusion of crop class and the application of importance weighting. Specific crop classes, including grains, legumes, and canola, showed accuracy improvements of 6, 42, and 13 percentage points, respectively. While the model showed promising results, limitations such as overprediction of no-tillage in non-grain crop classes were identified, pointing to the need for further refinement with additional data. The model was applied to map tillage practices across dryland fields in eastern Washington State for the years 2012, 2017, and 2022, and the predictions were generally congruent with the USDA Census of Agriculture county-level statistics. This research offers a scalable tool for the verification of conservation tillage practice adoption, supporting both regional and global monitoring efforts for sustainable agricultural practices.

Abstract (英文): Tidal marsh changes encompass both categorical cover change (i.e., extent change) and condition change, such as alterations in vegetation composition and hydrological regime, which together regulate ecosystem resilience and carbon cycling. Yet, most existing products rely on selective image compositing under similar tidal conditions or statistical values over fixed intervals, followed by epoch-based comparisons. These approaches are mainly designed to capture differences in marsh extent, while condition change within the same tidal marsh cover type remains largely unresolved despite its ecological importance. Here, we present a tide-informed dense time-series framework, DEtection and Characterization of cOastal tiDal wEtlands change (DECODE v2), for continuous monitoring of tidal marsh extent and change (including condition change and cover change, such as tidal marsh gain or loss) across the conterminous United States from 1988 to 2023. Two key methodological advances underpin this framework and enable global transferability: integration of a global empirical ocean tide model to estimate water levels for each Landsat observation, and an adaptive temporal normalization strategy to ensure consistent observation density across single- and dual-satellite eras. Stratified random sampling confirms strong performance in both tidal marsh extent and change mapping, supporting a shift from traditional binary extent mapping and extent-only change detection to a continuous monitoring strategy. Cross-product comparisons show that DECODE improves spatial consistency and reduces disagreement with reference interpretation for tidal marsh extent and cover change, while also providing additional information that is largely absent from existing products, including separation of cover change and condition change, change timing, and repeated change events at the same location. Critically, condition change dominated cumulative tidal marsh change across the conterminous United States (CONUS), accounting for approximately 81.1 ± 2.0% of the cumulative annual change area and greatly exceeding cumulative cover change (13,058 ± 1366 km 2 versus 3038 ± 216 km 2 ). By characterizing not only where tidal marsh extent changes occur, but also what type of change occurs, when it occurs, and how often it recurs, DECODE provides a more comprehensive basis for assessing ecosystem resilience, restoration effectiveness, and blue carbon dynamics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Current snow observations do not meet the data requirements established by the 2017 Decadal Survey and the Global Climate Observing System (GCOS) for global snow water equivalent (SWE) measurements. This paper evaluates existing spaceborne assets for global SWE and snow depth monitoring and identifies critical observational gaps that necessitate a dedicated snow observing strategy. We review snow data requirements across multiple application areas, including water resources, climate monitoring, weather forecasting, and disaster management, demonstrating the need for SWE measurements at 100 m to 4 km spatial resolution with 1- to 5-day temporal frequency and 10–20% accuracy. Analysis of current capabilities from missions including AMSR2, Sentinel-1, NISAR, and ICESat-2 reveals that while these sensors provide valuable SWE and depth information, they cannot, even when combined, deliver the comprehensive, high-resolution SWE data required for operational and scientific applications, observing only 40–60% of the global snowpack on a daily to weekly basis. We propose a global snow observing strategy centered on a core radar-based snow mission that would serve as the reference standard for a constellation of complementary sensors. This approach, combined with existing observations, ancillary data, advanced data fusion techniques, and land surface models, would provide accurate and timely SWE data essential for water resource management, climate research, and hazard mitigation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study investigates sub-pixel spatial variability in TROPOMI NO2 observations and its impact on the consistency between TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS observations for the Yeosu-Gwangyang industrial complex in South Korea. Furthermore, NOx emissions are estimated using both datasets and compared with a bottom-up emission inventory. The comparison of co-located TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS observations shows good correlation (r = 0.86), with TROPOMI data exhibiting a slight underestimation relative to the Mobile-DOAS observations (-8.5%). The underestimation was more pronounced when Mobile-DOAS observations had limited spatial coverage within the satellite pixel and was reduced when the spatial coverage increased, indicating the importance of spatial representativeness of ground-based measurements. The analysis of TROPOMI NO2 columns with the Exponentially Modified Gaussian (EMG) emission analysis reveals a decreasing trend in NOx emissions from 2021 to 2025 (-29%), along with pronounced seasonal variability. In comparison, Mobile-DOAS-derived emissions show substantial variability across individual measurement rounds and no clear seasonal pattern. A comparison of TROPOMI and Mobile-DOAS emission estimates with bottom-up emission inventory data indicates reasonable comparability, although differences vary across the observation periods. The weekend-to-weekday emission ratios range from 0.80 to 0.90, indicating a weak weekend effect consistent with the industrial characteristics of the region. Overall, this study highlights that (1) sub-pixel variability affects the consistency between satellite and ground-based observations and should be explicitly accounted for in satellite validation studies, and (2) satellite, Mobile-DOAS, and bottom-up estimates provide complementary information for quantifying NOx emissions over industrial regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The capability of the Chinese HY-2B/C/D altimeter constellation for gridded sea level anomaly (SLA) reconstruction using only HY-2 observations remains insufficiently evaluated. This study presents a physics-guided framework designed for near-real-time (NRT) SLA reconstruction in the Northwest Pacific and retrospectively evaluates it using 2022−2025 HY-2B/C/D observations. The framework combines an OI-derived coarse background with deep-learning refinement, with target-period reconstruction driven solely by HY-2B/C/D along-track data. Validation against an identical set of 1255 independent tide-gauge observations yields mean RMSE of 0.051 m and a mean correlation coefficient (r) of 0.83 over 12 tide-gauge stations. Inter-product comparisons during 2022−2025 show closer agreement with the DUACS DT and NRT products than with NSOAS, although differences in SLA reference conventions and geophysical correction standards among the products should be considered when interpreting regional discrepancies. Scale-dependent evaluation using SARAL/AltiKa along-track observations indicates a mean effective spatial resolution of approximately 300 km. Dynamical consistency is further assessed against surface-drifter observations using geostrophic velocity anomaly magnitude, yielding mean RMSE of 0.181 m s−1 and r of 0.54. These results demonstrate that spatially coherent and dynamically plausible daily SLA fields can be reconstructed with HY-2B/C/D as the sole altimetric observations during the target period, supporting the potential of the proposed framework for NRT SLA reconstruction under sparse observational constraints.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Global bathymetric products such as SRTM15+ provide extensive ocean coverage but cannot resolve many fine-scale seafloor features captured by shipborne multibeam surveys. Generative reconstruction methods can reconstruct more detailed terrain, but they may also introduce plausible-looking structures that are not supported by observations. We propose GravBathy-Diff, a conditional diffusion framework that uses observed low-resolution bathymetry as its primary input and incorporates satellite-altimetry-derived marine gravity as an auxiliary physical constraint. The gravity-differential enhancement module jointly encodes bathymetry, terrain derivatives, and the co-registered gravity field at the input stage. Gravity-conditioned time–frequency modulation regulates the contribution of gravity across diffusion stages and feature bands, while gravity-guided attention focuses this information on the spatial locations and feature channels where it is most useful. Deflections of the vertical, gravity anomalies, and vertical gravity gradients derived from Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) wide-swath altimetry are compared as alternative conditioning fields. Experiments are conducted on real SRTM15+ –multibeam bathymetry pairs from four ocean regions and assessed using depth error, perceptual similarity, terrain derivatives, and spectral fidelity. GravBathy-Diff achieves the strongest overall performance among the evaluated regression, adversarial, and diffusion methods, with the exception of one regional gradient-RMSE result. It ranks first on all six metrics averaged over four additional ocean areas and remains effective on an independent public digital elevation model (DEM) benchmark at 4×. Among the three gravity representations, the vertical gravity gradient provides the most effective guidance for reconstructing local relief. These results show that marine gravity can support the recovery of fine-scale seafloor structure while reducing high-frequency artifacts that are inconsistent with the available observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine oil spill remote sensing relies heavily on high-quality annotated data, yet the limited availability of well-annotated SAR oil spill samples with backscatter information and structural labels remains a challenge. To address the lack of structural consistency and scattering representation of existing generation methods, this study proposes a Semantic-Guided Diffusion for SAR Oil Spill Generation model (SGDiff-OS) for the joint generation of SAR oil spill backscatter coefficient σ° and corresponding binary oil spill labels, trained on a self-constructed SAR oil spill dataset. The model adopts a dual-channel conditional diffusion framework and incorporates semantic modulation to encode prior knowledge of spotted, blocky and slender morphologies, thereby improving structural representation and category controllability. Experimental results demonstrate that the generated samples show general agreement with real data in visual quality and overall statistical trends, although measurable distributional differences remain. The global mean σ° differs by approximately 1.96 dB between the real and generated samples. The generated samples retain the lower-backscatter relationship of oil-covered regions relative to seawater, although the oil-water contrast decreases from 6.59 dB to 4.65 dB. Furthermore, in an expert annotation evaluation of generated image mask pairs, the generated masks achieve an mIoU of 0.7658 and an F1-score of 0.8637 when evaluated against reference annotations. Under limited training data conditions, incorporating generated samples leads to a maximum improvement of 24.2% in mIoU, suggesting its potential value for sample augmentation under limited training conditions. These results demonstrate that the proposed model can generate SAR oil spill samples with the dominant scattering trends of real SAR data, providing supplementary data for oil spill detection and segmentation tasks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Surface Waves Investigation and Monitoring (SWIM) onboard the China-France Oceanography Satellite (CFOSAT) has acquired unprecedented global ocean wave direction spectrum information. Additionally, it incorporates a nadir observation mode for significant wave height (SWH) based on principles analogous to conventional altimeters, which serves to calibrate the total energy of the wave spectrum. The retrieval of SWH from such nadir radar observations generally employs theoretical models to retrack the waveforms reflected by the sea surface, a method that demonstrates high accuracy in the open ocean environment. However, obtaining SWH using traditional retracking algorithms is computationally expensive. Furthermore, in coastal regions, waveforms are affected by factors such as the presence of land and sea ice, which means that the SWH retrieval relying on theoretical models often either fails or yields substantial errors. Leveraging the fact that SWIM does not necessitate precise ranging, we bypassed the decades-old retracking paradigm by developing dual neural network inversion models. These models perform direct SWH estimation following an initial classification of SWIM nadir waveforms. The first model targets standard open-sea waveforms, while the second is designed for coastal and sea-ice 'ocean-like' waveforms that deviate from normal patterns yet retain essential wave information. The results show that the normal-ocean model achieves a processing speed over 13 times faster than the MLE4 algorithm and nearly 22 times faster than the adaptive retracking algorithm, while maintaining comparable accuracy (RMSE = 0.31 m) to the SWIM nadir SWH product. Meanwhile, the ocean-like model enhances both the quantity and quality of usable SWH data in non-open ocean areas, leveraging the accurate mapping relationships established by the neural networks. Generally, a rapid and robust scheme was developed in this paper for retrieving nadir SWH from CFOSAT SWIM, providing an alternative methodology for retrieving nadir geophysical parameters in future nadir-looking radar systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mapping small water body (SWB) dynamics at high spatiotemporal resolution remains challenging because optical imagery is frequently obscured by clouds, whereas SAR imagery is affected by speckle noise and blurred boundaries. Existing Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 fusion methods often have limited capability in fully exploiting the complementary information of the two sensors, leading to uncertainties in SWB mapping. Here we propose a deep learning-based method, the multiscale global-local water feature fusion network (MGLWNet), to map semi-monthly SWB dynamics via deep fusion of time-series Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 imagery. A large-scale dataset containing 1,193,347 SWBs (<10 ha) was constructed. MGLWNet incorporates a cloud detection branch to improve feature discrimination under cloud-contaminated conditions and a multiscale global-local feature module that combines atrous spatial pyramid pooling and multi-scale self-attention to capture both fine spatial details and global contextual information. When tested on cloud-free and cloud-contaminated datasets, MGLWNet outperformed Random Forest and several state-of-the-art deep learning models. Compared with Sentinel-1-based surface water mapping, the proposed fusion strategy substantially reduced commission errors, while the cloud detection branch effectively decreased omission errors in cloudy regions. MGLWNet was applied to map semi-monthly 10 m surface water dynamics in high-density SWB areas of Hubei Province (185,900 km2) over the period 2017–2024. Unbiased accuracy estimates were performed for validation. The semi-monthly surface water maps have a producer's accuracy of 87.65% (±2.50%) and user's accuracy of 93.11% (±2.62%), respectively, with a 95% confidence interval. Compared with Landsat-based products, the method captured more frequent intra-monthly SWB variations and detected more long-term changes (e.g. the appearance and disappearance of small ponds). The resulting high-temporal-resolution dataset further enabled characterization of multiple SWB change types, including stable seasonal water, water gain, water loss, dry periods, and wet periods. This study highlights the importance of deep learning and image fusion for characterizing spatiotemporal continuous SWB dynamics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Individual tree segmentation from terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) is fundamental to automated forest inventory, yet recent benchmarks have reached divergent conclusions about method performance. We evaluated six methods on TreeScanPL10K, a dataset of 10,417 manually segmented trees across 272 plots of diverse Central European forest. Two are algorithmic (treeX, RayExtract) and four are deep learning (TreeLearn, TreeAIBox, SegmentAnyTree, ForestFormer3D). Beyond aggregate accuracy, we characterized the per-method failure modes and the forest structural attributes that drive performance variation. A clear hierarchy emerged: ForestFormer3D achieved the highest mean IoU (0.76), followed by the other deep-learning methods (0.67–0.71) and the algorithmic approaches (0.60–0.62). Coniferous species were segmented more accurately than broadleaves (0.70 vs. 0.64), with the widest gap for the deep-learning methods. Higher accuracy came at substantial computational cost: 15 min/plot for ForestFormer3D versus under a minute for the fastest methods. To identify what makes a plot difficult to segment, we ran two complementary analyses: one on field-inventory attributes, the other on crown-geometry descriptors derived from the reference annotations. The dominant signal came from crown geometry: the plot-level Mean Crown Area Index (Mean CAI), which summarizes how many crown layers stack vertically above each ground cell, was the strongest single correlate and the top-ranked feature for every method individually. Multi-layered canopies are the hardest to segment. The field-inventory pool added a related but distinct dimension: structural diversity in species composition and spatial arrangement contributed beyond the vertical-layering signal. All six methods degraded in parallel along these gradients, indicating that the evaluated methods share common difficulties in structurally complex stands. We release the exact 2 cm benchmark point clouds, plot-level attributes and results, point-level predictions from all six methods for 18 selected plots, and the evaluation code.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hydrological connectivity is a fundamental regulator of material exchange, sediment transport, and ecological functioning in tidal flat ecosystems; yet, its long-term quantification remains challenging due to highly dynamic tidal inundation. Remote sensing provides an effective means for large-scale, multi-decadal assessments; however, tidal fluctuations and spectral ambiguity in intertidal zones often hinder the accurate extraction of tidal creek morphology. To address these limitations, this study developed an automated remote sensing classification framework integrating the Maximum spectral index composite (MSIC) and the Otsu algorithm to reconstruct a tide-normalized and spatiotemporally comparable tidal creek and landscape dataset from Landsat imagery (1990–2023) in the Nanliu River estuary, China. We then quantified hydrological connectivity based on the extracted tidal creek morphology and further examined its drivers by linking connectivity dynamics with long-term landscape pattern evolution. Results showed that hydrological connectivity in the Hepu intertidal zone exhibited a long-term decline despite an overall increase in tidal creek morphological complexity. Although total creek length and density increased from 15,479.89 m to 21,606.76 m and from 110.57 m/hm2 to 154.33 m/hm2, graph-based metrics revealed a long-term decline in structional connectivity. Vegetation expansion, particularly the rapid spread of Spartina alterniflora, is identified as a key factor associated with restricted flow pathways and accelerates the transformation from a landscape dominated by water and mudflats to one dominated by vegetation. This study offers new insights into how vegetation dynamics reshape hydrological connectivity in intertidal systems, providing an evidence base for understanding ecosystem evolution and informing coastal restoration and invasive species management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Near-surface air temperature (NSAT) is needed at both high spatial and high temporal resolution, but no single satellite sensor provides both. This study develops a clear-sky framework that estimates NSAT in near real time on a 30 m grid at 30-min intervals by fusing Geostationary Korea Multi-Purpose Satellite-2A (GK-2A) and Landsat-8 observations. A Taylor expansion-based downscaling algorithm was adapted to update its parameters at every GK-2A scan, and a thermodynamics-based formulation was simplified into an empirical regression of NSAT on land surface temperature (LST), longwave radiation and a moisture proxy derived from the normalized difference moisture index. Its three coefficients are recalibrated every hour against automatic weather station observations. Over southeastern South Korea in 2023, the root-mean-square error (RMSE) was 2.38 °C at stations withheld from calibration and 2.22 °C at the calibration stations, and it increased to 2.82 °C with previous-day coefficients. The downscaling reduced LST errors against Landsat-8 in 14 of 18 scenes but did not measurably change the station-scale NSAT accuracy. The 30 m grid therefore represents surface-type contrasts within each 2 km GK-2A pixel rather than independent thermal information at 30 m, and the product is valid only under clear-sky conditions with real-time AWS data available for hourly recalibration. Each half-hourly field was produced in about 30 s, supporting near-real-time operation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The number and diversity of thermal infrared observing systems have grown rapidly. This non-exhaustive review compiles an inventory of 185 named thermal infrared instrument designs spanning 390 historical, operational and planned satellite platform deployments. A status audit identified 83 of these deployments as operational on 18 August 2026. Across this orbital record, the finest reported nominal spatial sampling decreased from 55 km for the Medium Resolution Infrared Radiometer aboard TIROS-2 in 1960 to a provider-reported 3.5 m for the mid-wave infrared imager aboard HotSat-2 in 2026. This reduction of more than four orders of magnitude in nominal spatial sampling does not by itself establish improved effective spatial resolution or radiometric performance. Public continuity missions are now complemented by specialized instruments on the International Space Station, commercial small satellites, aircraft, stratospheric balloons, drones and terrestrial systems. This review links these platform histories to the governing physics of emitted radiation, detector and cooling technologies, calibration, atmospheric effects, emissivity and spatial resolution. It also examines the development from classical machine learning to convolutional, recurrent, transformer, diffusion, foundation and vision–language approaches. The selected examples illustrate different levels of adoption across thermal applications rather than a field-wide measure of uptake. These methods support image interpretation, reconstruction and quantitative retrieval, but model-inferred thermal detail, however visually convincing, is not a direct measurement and must be identified and validated as such. As sensor availability expands, scientific comparability increasingly depends on harmonization, cross-sensor transfer, uncertainty characterization and validation in physical units. We recommend three priorities: (i) open, cross-calibrated thermal archives; (ii) models that preserve the distinct physical meanings of thermal variables; and (iii) validation across sensors, regions and seasons using physical units, independent observations and quantified uncertainty.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Analysing the spatial and temporal variability of near-surface air temperature (Ta) is essential for assessing climate change, particularly in polar regions such as the maritime Antarctic, where observational studies remain comparatively scarce. In areas with sparse meteorological networks, satellite observations provide a valuable means of extending station-based analyses and generating spatially distributed Ta maps. This study analyses updated Ta records from six meteorological stations on the Hurd Peninsula, Livingston Island, for 2000–2024, with particular attention to temperature changes since 2016, and evaluates Ta estimation models based on MODIS Terra daytime LST Collection 6.1 data. The analysis of the Ta time series showed significant warming over the complete 2000–2024 period, with trends ranging from 0.53 to 1.27 °C decade−1, and a clear shift towards warming after 2016. While the 2000–2015 period showed spatially contrasting trends, including cooling at JCI, positive trends were observed at all analysed stations during 2016–2024, ranging from 0.40 to 1.33 °C decade−1 and highlighting substantial local variability across the Hurd Peninsula. The global Ta model, developed using data from all six stations and based on MODIS Terra daytime LST and spatiotemporal variables, achieved a performance comparable to that obtained with Collection 6, with R2 = 0.77 and a cross-validated RMSE of 2.23 °C. The results also showed that rigorous filtering of the MODIS LST observations is essential: retaining only good-quality data and excluding observations with |view angle| > 45° substantially reduced dispersion in the Ta–LST relationship and removed unrealistically low LST values. The introduction of this view-angle threshold represents a new filtering step with respect to our previous studies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): On 4 December 2025, an MW5.8 earthquake struck Aheqi County, Xinjiang. The epicenter is located approximately 24 km southwest of the 2024 Wushi MW7.0 earthquake, within the hinterland of the Kalpin Tagh fold-and-thrust belt on the western segment of the South Tianshan seismic zone. To clarify the seismogenic characteristics of this earthquake and its dynamic correlation with the strong Wushi MW7.0 earthquake, we constructed a time-series observation dataset covering 22 months before to 5 months after the earthquake based on ascending and descending Sentinel-1A SAR images, and extracted the time-series deformation trend of Aheqi 22 months before the earthquake (after the Wushi earthquake) using the SBAS-InSAR technology. A step term is introduced into the time-series model to eliminate local unwrapping errors and residual atmospheric delays caused by severe convective weather in the summer of June 2025, so as to obtain a reliable net LOS trend during the observation period. Combined with the co-seismic line-of-sight deformation field acquired by the two-pass D-InSAR method, a comprehensive study is carried out by integrating distributed fault slip inversion and Coulomb stress change simulation. The results show the following: (1) The co-seismic deformation is dominated by positive LOS displacement (motion toward the satellite), with maximum LOS deformations of approximately 5.0 cm and 6.0 cm for the ascending and descending orbits, respectively. The deformation field presents a significantly asymmetric elliptical shape with no surface rupture, indicating a blind thrust rupture. (2) SBAS time-series analysis for the 22 months preceding the earthquake reveals tectonic rates of approximately +26.9 mm/yr for the hanging wall and +8.5 mm/yr for the footwall, yielding a net across-fault LOS velocity difference over the observation window of ∼18.4 mm/yr. We note that the first SAR acquisition in our pre-seismic time series (February 2024) occurs only ∼12 days after the 2024 Wushi Mw7.0 earthquake (22 January 2024). Consequently, the estimated linear trend inherently captures a superposition of possible early post-seismic transient deformation and interseismic tectonic loading. We therefore interpret the ∼18.4 mm/yr difference as the net across-fault LOS velocity difference over the observation window. This value is compatible with, but does not independently verify, long-term elastic strain accumulation on the Aheqi fault. (3) The inverted seismogenic fault has a strike of approximately 242°, a dip angle of approximately 40°, a slip angle of approximately 74°, a centroid depth of approximately 6.2 km, a maximum slip of approximately 0.17 m, and a moment magnitude of MW5.9. The rupture is dominated by thrust motion with a minor strike-slip component, the exact direction of which is not fully constrained, indicating that the Aheqi fault is the likely seismogenic structure of this earthquake. (4) The Coulomb stress calculation results show that the 2024 Wushi MW7.0 earthquake produced a static stress loading of more than 2 bar in the source region of the Aheqi fault, far exceeding the earthquake empirical reference threshold of 0.1 bar. Combining the pre-seismic strain accumulation characteristics, co-seismic slip distribution and Coulomb stress calculation results, we suggest that the Aheqi earthquake—a moderate blind thrust event—may have been promoted by static stress loading from the 2024 Wushi MW7.0 earthquake, superimposed on long-term tectonic loading. While the exact time-dependent triggering process (including the 22-month delay) requires further viscoelastic or pore-pressure diffusion modeling, the static Coulomb stress analysis confirms a positive loading of >2 bar. The results of this study can provide measured deformation constraints and reference for analyzing the seismogenic mechanism of the structural transition zone in the piedmont of South Tianshan and improving the regional seismotectonic model.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban fringe areas are highly heterogeneous transition zones where rapid land-use change intensifies conflicts among food production, water regulation, and ecological functions. This study integrated remote-sensing products with meteorological, soil, terrain, and socioeconomic data to assess ecosystem services (ES) trade-off optimization potential (TOP) and identify candidate spatial priorities across the urban fringe areas of Henan Province, China, under 2022 baseline conditions. We used a production possibility frontier–Euclidean distance (PPF–ED) framework to quantify TOP as the shortest Euclidean distance to a fitted empirical Pareto frontier in standardized ecosystem-service space. We fitted the frontiers to selected pairwise combinations of estimated grain production (GP), carbon storage (CS), soil retention (SR), and water yield (WY). TOP measures relative geometric departure from these frontiers. Attainable gains in service supply and the benefits of specific management interventions require separate assessment. A ten-predictor Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) model, combined with Shapley additive explanations (SHAP) and multidimensional partial dependence plots (PDPs), identified key predictors and characterized nonlinear individual and joint response patterns. GP–WY showed the strongest negative association and the largest regional distance to its empirical frontier (D = 0.536). Gross domestic product (GDP) and patch density were the leading predictors, accounting for 20.9% and 20.8% of relative mean absolute SHAP importance, respectively, followed by nighttime light (15.6%). Multidimensional PDPs showed higher predicted TOP under combinations of elevated socioeconomic intensity and landscape fragmentation. Integrating PDP-derived high-response conditions, positive local SHAP contributions, and observed high-TOP areas identified candidate priority zones mainly in the northern, northeastern, and central urban fringe areas. The framework connects distance-based trade-off assessment, nonlinear response interpretation, and spatial prioritization to support differentiated ecosystem management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite video enables continuous observation over wide areas, capturing spatiotemporal information for dynamic airborne target monitoring and aviation activity analysis. Such information facilitates aircraft localization and continuous trajectory tracking and can further support finer-grained aircraft analysis. However, publicly available satellite video datasets with in-flight aircraft trajectories remain scarce, and existing data are largely concentrated on airport-centered observations, with limited coverage of wide-area scenes due to the difficulty of data acquisition and the high cost of trajectory annotation. To address this problem, we introduce SAVAir, a satellite video dataset that combines real remote sensing backgrounds with both real and synthetic aircraft instances. SAVAir contains 1281 videos and 99,960 frames, with 1,284,563 horizontal bounding box (HBB) annotations and 159,045 oriented bounding box (OBB) labels. Moreover, the dataset provides rich information, such as visible-instance masks, aircraft configurations, structural attributes, flight phases, and land-cover descriptions. The synthetic data generation pipeline integrates 3D structural modeling, illumination and material reflectance simulation, atmospheric radiative transfer, optical degradation, and motion dynamics, together with sequence-level product-domain calibration and trajectory constraints, to maintain cross-frame appearance and motion consistency. To the best of our knowledge, the proposed pipeline is the first physics-guided synthesis approach specifically designed for aircraft in satellite videos. The synthesized targets achieve an LPIPS score of 0.1607, lower than Copy-Paste (0.1925), SinGAN (0.1943), and Stable Diffusion (0.2112), under consistent experimental settings. Representative object detectors and multi-object trackers are further evaluated under a unified benchmark protocol, while source-disjoint transfer experiments demonstrate the utility of synthetic pretraining under limited real supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Inverse synthetic aperture radar (ISAR) is important for space target surveillance and recognition. However, under large rotation angles and uniformly accelerated target rotation, coupled high-order migration through range cells (MTRC) and two-dimensional space-variant phase errors cannot be accurately compensated by conventional methods based on small-angle or constant-rotation assumptions, causing image defocusing. To address this, a physics-driven subaperture differentiable back-projection (BP) ISAR imaging network is proposed. It embeds a parametric rotation model with angular velocity and acceleration: taking complex-valued HRRP sequences as input, the full aperture is partitioned into overlapping subapertures, and rotation parameters are regressed by a cross-subaperture parameter estimation network exploiting local scattering structures, temporal correlations, and motion evolution features; a differentiable BP imaging layer unifies rotation transformation, slant-range computation, complex-valued interpolation, phase compensation, and coherent accumulation into differentiable tensor operations, allowing image-domain losses to backpropagate to the parameter estimation module for closed-loop joint optimization of parameter estimation and focusing quality; and a phase registration and quality-guided coherent fusion mechanism adaptively suppresses sidelobes, defocusing, and noise from low-quality subapertures. Experiments on point-scatterer and electromagnetic simulation models demonstrate clear advantages, particularly at low SNR, providing a new path for high-resolution ISAR imaging of large-angle maneuvering targets.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Surface solar radiation (SSR) over East Asia has been studied mainly over land, although aerosols and clouds may change differently over the adjacent seas. Using the four sky states of the Clouds and the Earth’s Radiant Energy System (CERES) SYN1deg product for 2004–2021, we decompose the all-sky SSR exactly into aerosol, cloud, and background contributions and compare land with ocean on the same grid. All-sky SSR increased over the ocean at +2.68 W m−2 decade−1 but showed no significant trend over land. The aerosol contribution was positive on both surfaces and significant over 61% of land and 92% of ocean cells, whereas the all-sky SSR trend was significant over only 8% of land cells. Over land, the cloud and background contributions offset most of the aerosol contribution. The ocean-minus-land difference in the SSR trend, +2.26 W m−2 decade−1, comprises contributions of −0.91 from aerosol, +2.75 from cloud, and +0.42 W m−2 decade−1 from the background. Aerosol optical depth (AOD) declined 1.7 times faster over land, whereas the clear-sky flux over the ocean is only 1.2 times as sensitive to AOD, so the aerosol contribution is larger over land (+2.61 versus +1.70 W m−2 decade−1). Within the cloud contribution, the difference arises from the optical thickness part (−1.13 over land versus +1.52 W m−2 decade−1 over the ocean), not from the cloud amount part (−0.47 versus −0.41 W m−2 decade−1).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine ecological restoration projects are commonly expected to enhance sediment retention and improve coastal stability, but the morphological changes observed on tidal flats during such works may differ markedly between sectors. This study examined short-term changes in the morphology and vegetation of the Nanhui tidal flat, Shanghai, over a period that coincided with the Lingang marine ecological restoration project, from 2021 to September 2024. Multi-source satellite imagery, CoastSat-based waterline extraction, waterline-derived topographic reconstruction, an unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-derived reference surface, and vegetation-index analysis were combined to quantify shoreline movement, tidal-flat area, intertidal slope, cross-sectional elevation change, and vegetation recovery. The reconstructed topography agreed with UAV-derived elevations, with an RMSE of 0.17 m and an R2 of 0.84. Shoreline change rates measured from images acquired at nearly identical water levels ranged from −4.10 to +5.47 m yr−1. The annual mean intertidal gradient was 0.161° in 2021, 0.180° in 2022, 0.177° in 2023 and 0.170° in 2024, with year-to-year differences close to the propagated uncertainty of the gradient; approximately 60% of the monitored sections showed net erosion. At the same time, localized accretion and vegetation recovery occurred in adjacent sectors. These results illustrate the sector-dependent morphodynamic adjustment of engineered tidal flats and show that satellite-derived topography can resolve short-term erosion-accretion patterns in rapidly changing estuarine-coastal settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral LiDAR (HSL) has emerged as a cutting-edge technique for underwater 3D imaging. Nevertheless, wavelength-selective absorption and scattering of light by water induce severe distortion in the spectral information of submerged targets and further result in color casts. In this paper, the underwater target spectral radiometric correction (UWSRC) method is adopted to correct the spectral reflectance of underwater targets by combining pre-measured water spectral transmittance and range data acquired by LiDAR. This approach obviates the complicated in situ measurement of water inherent optical parameters, which reduces the operational complexity for field deployments of underwater hyperspectral LiDAR remote sensing. Its spectral correction capability is experimentally validated under varying detection distances and diverse aquatic environments, including clear tap water and eutrophic turbid water. Furthermore, a dedicated HSL prototype for underwater scanning and a semi-submerged pod are developed. With this prototype, the 3D scanning imaging distance can be extended to 5.3 m in an outdoor swimming pool. Experimental results demonstrate that the UWSRC method can effectively compensate for the spectral reflectance of underwater targets under different water qualities and detection ranges. Meanwhile, true-color 3D point clouds of a color-calibration board are successfully obtained during long-distance scanning. Error analysis of reflectance inversion is carried out to explore the sensitivity to water transmittance bias and ranging uncertainty. The experimental investigations in this paper verify that the UWSRC method achieves favorable performance in underwater target spectral restoration and true-color reconstruction across different water qualities and detection distances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Invasive plant species pose major ecological and economic challenges, with effective management dependent on accurate and timely detection. This study developed and evaluated a workflow for detecting flowering-stage Chromolaena odorata (Siam Weed) using high-resolution UAV (uncrewed aerial vehicle) imagery and a YOLOv11 object detection model. UAV images captured at Labelle Downs in the Northern Territory were processed into an orthomosaic and tiled for annotation and training. After excluding training tiles, sub-tile-level assessment yielded precision of 0.99, recall of 0.77 and an F1-score of 0.86; validation tiles remained included, so this was not a fully independent test. Detection maps represented candidate infestation patterns. Model transferability was evaluated without retraining at an independent site near Townsville, Queensland, where precision was 0.76, recall was 0.82 and F1-score was 0.79. A supplementary pooled analysis excluding training imagery found lower precision in the highest ranked near-white-pixel group (0.77) than in the remaining classes (0.98), but could not separate brightness from site, sensor, vegetation and image-quality differences. The workflow provides spatially coherent outputs that could guide field verification and management. Further validation across sites, seasons and phenological stages is needed to establish broader applicability.

Abstract (英文): Urban development involves both the expansion of built-up areas and changes in urban activity intensity. Using nighttime light (NTL) and built-up land (BUL) data from 2002 to 2022, this study examined built-up area expansion, changes in nighttime activity, and transitions between development patterns across two periods in the Sichuan–Chongqing region at a 500 m grid resolution. The results show that BUL increased by a net total of 4494.52 km2, while NTL intensity increased across a substantial share of the study area. The area of newly developed BUL was 18.08% greater in 2012–2022 than in 2002–2012, whereas the average expansion per grid cell decreased by 17.37%. Meanwhile, NTL increases became more widespread in the second period. Among areas characterized by spatial expansion in the first period, 28.84% shifted to a pattern of concurrent spatial expansion and NTL intensification in the second period, and 17.23% shifted to an NTL intensification pattern. These transitions indicate a clear difference in the timing of land development and urban activity growth. Chengdu showed particularly pronounced NTL intensification in the second period, whereas Chongqing exhibited multiple development pathways, with spatial expansion, concurrent growth, and NTL intensification occurring across different areas. These findings help identify how built-up areas and urban activity evolve along different pathways at different stages of rapid urbanization.

Abstract (英文): LiDAR is an important remote sensing technology for acquiring three-dimensional spatial information, providing data for object identification, localization, and scene understanding in applications such as autonomous driving. As these systems rely on deep learning models, adversarial attacks have become an important tool for evaluating their robustness. Among attack paradigms, transfer-based attacks are particularly practical, crafting adversarial point clouds on accessible surrogate models and transferring them to unknown target models. However, most existing methods either perturb all points under a uniform constraint or concentrate distortion on critical regions, ignoring spatial variations in geometry and sensitivity and often damaging the structures that recognition relies on. Moreover, they target object-level classification and transfer poorly to scene-level detection. Therefore, we propose MIST (Multi-source Imperceptible Saliency-guided Transferable), an adversarial attack framework for LiDAR point cloud perception in remote sensing. MIST fuses source-model gradients, local geometric variation, and predictive uncertainty into a multi-source saliency score that guides perturbation allocation. Guided by this score, Reverse control tightens displacement limits at high-saliency sites, while Intensity scaling modulates update strength to maintain attack effectiveness. Their coordination allocates the budget adaptively by saliency, improving cross-model transferability while preserving geometric imperceptibility. We evaluate MIST through classification on ModelNet40 and ScanObjectNN, and extend to full-scene 3D object detection on KITTI and Waymo under the same transfer-based setting. Results show MIST improves the transferability-fidelity balance in classification and achieves effective cross-model attacks in detection, offering insights for developing more robust perception models in LiDAR-based remote sensing.

</details>

XGBoost, a pure Transformer, and a hybrid Mamba–Transformer were compared under a common station-level split and 0% training masking. On 11,062 test records from 110 held-out stations, the hybrid model achieved a root mean square error (RMSE) of 0.0709. Paired station-bootstrap intervals for inter-model RMSE differences included zero. Retrained ablations increased the hybrid model’s RMSE to 0.1002 after removing nine neighborhood-mean features and to 0.1067 after removing five local standard-deviation features. Both types of neighborhood information therefore contributed predictive information under the evaluated setting. Additional diagnostics examined conditional errors, water-vapor substitution, target transformation, and compositing order. Estimates for sparsely sampled surface types and high-AOD conditions remained uncertain. A China-wide map illustrated model response on a nominal approximately 1 km grid. Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of crop evapotranspiration (ETc) plays an important role in sustainable agricultural water management, precision irrigation planning, and food security under a dynamically changing climate. Physics-based models such as the FAO-56 Penman–Monteith equation have a sound theoretical background but are limited in modeling real-time dynamics in vegetation, owing to their use of static crop coefficient (Kc) tables. Meanwhile, ML models trained between the satellite-derived vegetation indices and crop coefficients are prone to physical bound violations and poor spatial generalizability. In this study, the Physics-Informed Machine Learning (PIML) framework is introduced, which consists of connecting the data from Sentinel-2 multispectral reflectance with the data from MODIS gap-filled time-series and the thermal-based reference evapotranspiration by NASA ECOSTRESS within a hard-bounded residual structure, Kc = clip(Kc, prior + clip(fθ(x), −0.15, 0.15), 0.15, 1.20), where the neural network’s correction term resides between FAO-56 agronomic bounds. Although the held-out validation set (N = 200) was intentionally kept small due to the limited number of cloud-free ECOSTRESS overpasses, an operational telemetry system recorded hourly data from four crop fields at the Sustainable Agriculture Facility (RSRF) in California. Because the same four fields and 25 sectors are visited across both training and testing periods, the experimental evaluation represents an intra-site, short-term temporal forward-transfer test rather than a true spatial generalization. The limited sample size (five cloud-free dates) precluded true spatial generalization methods such as leave-one-field-out or field-blocked cross-validation, so we kept all four crops in the training set. Satellite data for the same field are highly spatially correlated; therefore, we used the ICC (intraclass correlation coefficient) to adjust for non-independence, reducing N = 200 to neff =12.0. Three models were benchmarked: an empirical baseline with an RMSE = 0.3801, a constrained PIML network (RMSE = 0.3263), and a global training-mean predictor (RMSE = 0.3257). Model B removed all bound violations in the physical domain (compared to 14% for Model A), reduced RMSE by 14.1% from the baseline, and produced results similar to the global mean; the design-effect-corrected p-value (0.5492) confirms the pilot comparison is statistically underpowered. When validating remote sensing applications with small, clustered samples, these results show that hard physical constraints eliminate physically implausible predictions during temporal forward projection, while highlighting the need to correct spatial autocorrelation and requiring dedicated field-blocked evaluations for future claims of spatial generalization.

</details>
