Feature instances representing fault-location evidence, geometric measurements, LOS deformation patterns, and geomorphological responses were mapped to fault-object or fault-segment levels and linked to supporting records. A directed acyclic graph (DAG) records processing dependencies and identifies downstream nodes affected by source-data or parameter changes. Changes in interpretation criteria or manually interpreted feature boundaries are linked to corresponding instances and attributes for manual review. A thermal-width update experiment identified affected records, revised three segment-level thermal-width attributes, retained predefined nonthermal controls, and preserved provenance. An equivalent relational implementation reproduced all six updated width estimates with a maximum absolute difference of 0 m. FRSDO provides a domain-specific framework for fault-feature representation, evidence linkage, provenance, and controlled dependency-based updating.

Abstract (英文): Aerial object detectors must resolve small, closely spaced objects while retaining enough context to recognize large structures. RS-Adapter uses an image-level class prior to update intermediate features in a frozen vision transformer. The prior combines a learned classifier, fixed class prototypes, and class-conditional descriptor statistics. Cross-attention links the prior to local features, which are fused across scales and gated before being added to the transformer. The adapted features guide a denoiser that generates box prompts for the detection decoder. On VisDrone, RS-Adapter achieved 39.2% average precision (AP) and 28.4% small-object AP, 3.1 percentage points above DEIM-D-FINE-L on the latter metric. AP50 was 78.3% on DIOR, 84.5% on NWPU VHR-10, and 87.7% on RSOD. RS-Adapter trained 16.8 million of its 103.2 million parameters and used less peak training memory than full tuning in the VisDrone comparison.

Abstract (英文): In microwave coincidence imaging (MCI), radiation patterns with low mutual correlation, generated by an appropriately selected frequency set, provide spatial diversity for high-resolution imaging. For moving targets, however, residual state-prediction errors after motion compensation can still cause a mismatch between the echoes and the observation matrix, degrading imaging performance despite this spatial diversity. To improve imaging robustness under this mismatch, frequency-set selection is guided by a conditional upper bound on the scattering-coefficient recovery error. This bound accounts for both the sensitivity of the observation matrix to state-prediction errors and the distinguishability of sparse scatterers. The state-prediction-error tolerance is chosen such that this bound does not exceed a prescribed recovery-error threshold. The frequency set is then selected by maximizing the minimum tolerance over the prescribed observation conditions. The resulting frequency set is used for closed-loop tracking and imaging with a frequency-diverse metasurface. During closed-loop operation, the predicted state is used to form the observation matrix, and the image centroid is fed back to update the state estimate. This update incorporates the correlation between state-prediction errors and image-derived observation errors. Simulation results show that, at a signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of 10dB and an x-direction offset of 0.08m, the success rate for resolving two targets separated by 1.83∘ increases from 20% with uniform frequency sampling to 80% with the selected frequency set. For an extended target at 0dB SNR, the peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), evaluated against a stationary-target reference reconstruction, improves by 1.5dB, and the scatterer-localization root-mean-square error (RMSE) for matched detections decreases by 71.8% compared with uniform frequency sampling. These results demonstrate that the proposed criterion improves resolution, image quality, and localization accuracy in the presence of state-prediction errors, without increasing the number of operating frequencies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Removing topographic effects from optical satellite imagery is essential for monitoring vegetation in mountainous regions, because uneven solar illumination causes substantial reflectance variation within the same land-cover class. Existing topographic correction tools vary in method coverage, solar-geometry handling, and sensor or data-format requirements. This study presents fortocorrpy, an open-source Python package providing topographic correction methods across optical satellite sensors. The package implements six methods through a common interface: cosine, C-correction, SCS, SCS + C, Statistical–Empirical (SE), and Empirical Rotation (ER). It automatically aligns ancillary rasters to the image grid and calculates illumination conditions for each pixel. In an evaluation against reference images generated using a radiative transfer model, ER yielded the smallest error in both summer and winter, whereas cosine and SCS became unstable under low solar elevation. In Harmonized Landsat and Sentinel-2 (HLS) time series, ER removed most of the difference in near-infrared reflectance of vegetation (NIRv) between opposing forest slopes while leaving flat cropland unchanged. In a comparison of processing time with five existing topographic correction tools, fortocorrpy was the fastest. By also returning intermediate products such as cos i and fitted coefficients, fortocorrpy supports topographic correction across optical satellite datasets and new method development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Traditional fractional vegetation cover (FVC) calculation methods relying solely on either airborne LiDAR waveforms or point clouds often suffer from degraded performance due to single-modality limitations and spatially uneven point density. To address this issue, this study proposes a multimodal fusion network termed the joint LiDAR waveform and point-cloud network (JWP-Net) and a 2D grid area ratio-based FVC calculation method. First, with a high-resolution digital orthophoto map as an auxiliary reference, an interactive human–machine visual interpretation workflow is adopted to precisely label LiDAR echoes into three categories: vegetation, land, and water. A standardized dataset is thereby established for subsequent data analysis and model training. Second, a novel deep learning network termed JWP-Net, which utilizes LiDAR echo waveforms and point clouds as multimodal inputs, is proposed to classify the laser points into vegetation, land, and water. Third, a 2D grid area ratio-based FVC calculation method is proposed to calculate the FVC based on the classified laser points. To validate the effectiveness and superiority of the proposed framework, experiments are conducted using raw measurement data acquired by the Optech Aquarius LiDAR system. Experimental results demonstrate that JWP-Net achieves an overall classification accuracy of 94.00% ± 0.41% (mean value ± standard deviation of 5-fold overall accuracies evaluated on the untouched held-out test grids), outperforming single-modal models by 1.77–2.52%. Furthermore, integrating DBSCAN-based spatial morphological smoothing on the held-out grids steadily improves the overall classification accuracy to 94.81% ± 0.44%. Moreover, compared with the traditional laser point-count-based FVC calculation method, the proposed 2D grid area ratio-based method reduces the FVC absolute deviation by 3 percentage points on test grids at a 0.2 m grid resolution. The multimodal fusion strategy that combines airborne laser full-waveform and point-cloud data, and the 2D grid area-ratio-based FVC calculation method proposed in this work, offer a novel research approach for high-precision FVC calculation using full-waveform LiDAR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Gas-absent thermal references determine the contrast available for ground-based Fourier transform infrared (FTIR) gas identification, but dense reference sampling increases the measurement burden. We examine compact reference libraries using measured dual-detector interferograms from methane, carbon monoxide, and physically acquired mixtures. Frequency-dependent Planck interpolation and detector-specific scalar approximations are compared with simpler reference substitution using fixed classifiers, measured-reference controls, and acquisition-grouped evaluation. Retaining two endpoint measurements per date/resolution stratum reduces the background library from 48 to 12 acquisitions; across 36 omitted interior backgrounds, Planck interpolation lowers the mean relative waveform error from 0.5512 to 0.1794 for InSb and from 0.1177 to 0.0837 for MCT relative to temperature-linear interpolation. On 54 gas acquisitions, the exact-scene error falls from 52.5% to 23.5% with ExtraTrees and from 53.1% to 29.0% with the neural evaluator; reanalysis using all 65,976 background interferogram rows gives the same trend. Scalar weighting and larger reference libraries produce classifier-dependent outcomes, showing that both the waveform error and gas scene error are needed to select a compact thermal reference library.

Abstract (英文): Sparse lower-tropospheric wind observations over the ocean remain an important source of uncertainty in tropical cyclone (TC) track prediction. This proof-of-concept case study examines lower-tropospheric wind profiles collected by a wave glider equipped with Doppler wind lidar over the northern South China Sea during Typhoon KRATHON (2024). The quality-controlled profiles were assimilated into the Weather Research and Forecasting model using hourly three-dimensional variational rapid update cycling (RUC). Two 72 h forecasts were compared: a control experiment without the wave-glider observations (CTRL) and an assimilation experiment with the wave-glider wind profiles (DA_WGLD). DA_WGLD reduced the 6–72 h mean track error from 75 to 56 km, the root-mean-square error from 83 to 59 km, and the maximum error from 176 to 89 km. The main separation occurred after 48 h, when CTRL developed an increasing northward and eastward displacement, whereas DA_WGLD remained closer to the IBTrACS best track. Initial wind-analysis differences showed coherent spatial structure near the glider and the storm environment. From 42 to 66 h, the northward component of the diagnosed deep-layer steering flow was generally weaker in DA_WGLD, consistent with the reduced northeastward displacement after recurvature. The results establish the feasibility of assimilating wave-glider wind profiles in hourly RUC and quantify the track forecast improvement observed for KRATHON under the selected configuration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Groundwater constitutes a vital component of the water resources of the Mongolian Plateau (MP), supplying water for industrial, agricultural, domestic, and ecological uses while sustaining the local ecosystems. However, the long-term patterns of groundwater storage change and their environmental associations remain incompletely understood. In this study, we quantified groundwater storage anomalies (GWSA) from 2003 to 2023 using data from the GRACE/GRACE-FO gravity satellites and the Global Land Data Assimilation System (GLDAS), which revealed a marked regional decline of 0.322 cm yr−1. By employing a random forest model that accounts for both climate-related and human-induced factors, we identified precipitation (Pre), surface runoff (SurR), potential evapotranspiration (PET), and vegetation greening (increases in NDVI) as the key drivers of groundwater depletion. Further analysis through multiple linear regression revealed the spatially varying impacts of Pre, SurR, and PET, with groundwater changes in western Mongolia and southern Inner Mongolia exhibiting strong spatial correlations with PET. In southern Inner Mongolia—a desert region and a focal area of China-led large-scale ecological restoration—this association likely reflects the increased PET and the ecological water use sourced from groundwater, both induced by vegetation restoration efforts. These findings indicate that large-scale vegetation restoration in water-limited regions could significantly strain groundwater resources. Therefore, integrating groundwater management into ecological restoration strategies is essential for sustaining the long-term resilience of both ecosystems and regional environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing change detection (CD) is commonly formulated as pixel-level localization of predefined changes or changes specified by explicit semantic categories. However, practical users often express their requirements indirectly through functional, causal, or consequence-oriented descriptions, creating a semantic gap between natural-language intent and the corresponding spatiotemporal change regions. Our introduces implicit user-intent-guided change detection and proposes IntentCD, an intent-aware multimodal framework that translates indirect user requirements into pixel-level change maps. The key idea is to use a latent change-of-interest representation to bridge intent interpretation and temporal change localization. Specifically, bi-temporal remote sensing images and user instructions are jointly encoded into a multimodal representation, while a pretrained large language model is employed to infer implicit user intent from implicit descriptions. The inferred semantic representation is then used to condition a change-map decoder, which integrates high-level semantic features and multi-scale visual features for pixel-level change localization. Experiments are conducted on the BCDD and SECOND datasets. On BCDD, IntentCD achieves an F1 score of 92.1% for building change detection. In addition, implicit-intent annotations derived from multiple land-cover categories in SECOND are used to evaluate the model under indirect semantic descriptions. The results demonstrate that the proposed framework can generate change maps conditioned on both explicit and implicit user requirements while maintaining competitive change detection performance. These findings suggest that incorporating implicit user intent provides a more flexible interface between natural-language requirements and remote sensing change analysis.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation dynamics in heterogeneous mountain–plain transition zones may be represented differently by satellite sensors with contrasting spatial and temporal characteristics. This study evaluated NDVI derived from Landsat, MODIS, and Sentinel-2 in the Northern Qinling Piedmont, China, and investigated vegetation changes across complementary spatiotemporal scales. The three datasets showed substantial spatial and seasonal consistency, while differences in NDVI magnitude, short-term temporal variations, and data completeness were also observed. Although the original monthly time series exhibited high inter-sensor correlations, these correlations decreased substantially after removing seasonal effects, indicating that shared seasonal variations accounted for much of the apparent temporal agreement. Sentinel-2 and MODIS exhibited higher data availability than Landsat during 2021–2025. Considering its continuous long-term record and relatively high data availability, MODIS was used to characterize long-term vegetation changes during 2001–2025, while Sentinel-2 was used to resolve recent fine-scale changes during 2019–2025. Regional NDVI increased significantly, with pronounced greening mainly in the eastern and southern areas. For the long-term record, declining or non-significant trends were concentrated mainly in urbanized areas, whereas recent Sentinel-2 observations revealed localized NDVI declines in the western Qinling piedmont floodplain. NDVI was positively associated with temperature and precipitation, with stronger relationships in spring. Precipitation showed its strongest positive association with NDVI over a three-month accumulation window. Topographic conditions and land-use/land-cover changes were also associated with spatial and temporal heterogeneity in vegetation dynamics. These results show that broad regional greening can coexist with localized vegetation decline and highlight the importance of matching satellite sensor characteristics with monitoring scales in heterogeneous ecological transition zones.

</details>

The all-six-fold POD–QR minimum was 14, 10, 5, and 3 retained levels at RMSE tolerances of 0.30, 0.40, 0.50 and 0.70 m s−1, respectively. Abstract (英文): Polarimetric SAR (PolSAR) records multiple polarization channels to characterize target scattering, but its coherent imaging mechanism produces multiplicative speckle that complicates downstream analysis. Existing despeckling methods pursue different objectives, so visual inspection and conventional image-quality metrics may not reflect their practical utility. In this work, six representative conventional and deep-learning-based despeckling methods and the unfiltered data are evaluated through three downstream tasks: land-cover classification on three scenes, ice-type classification on a GaoFen-3 scene, and CFAR-based ship detection on offshore images of the FPSD dataset. Each task depends on a different property of the filtered covariance data, so the same despeckled product can suit one task and not another: NL-SAR gives the highest land-cover accuracy on two scenes but ranks sixth of seven inputs in ship detection, whereas SDNLM attains the highest calibrated detection F1 but ranks sixth of seven on the Island scene. This task dependence persists under targeted sensitivity analyses of smoothing strength, classifier choice, covariance validity, and detector calibration. Despeckling methods should therefore be evaluated against the information that the intended downstream task requires and selected on that basis, rather than by visual quality or a single task-independent criterion.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Foliar biostimulants are widely used in sunflower to mitigate abiotic stress, but their on-farm evaluation is hampered by unreplicated strip designs and field variability. Multispectral UAV images were acquired on the day of treatment, at flowering and at the yellow-back-of-head stage, and NDVI, NDRE, GNDVI and LCI were analysed for 12 digital sampling boxes per strip with baseline-referenced statistics, repeated-measures ANOVA and Cohen’s d. At treatment, no differences between the strips were detected (p ≥ 0.39). Thereafter, they diverged in the order Dell Agro plus > Natural Force > Auronik > Amalgerol: the change in GNDVI to flowering differed among strips (p = 0.002; Holm-adjusted p = 0.013), and at the final stage Dell Agro plus exceeded Amalgerol by 15.5% in NDVI (d = 1.07–1.31 across indices), retained the most greenness and showed the smallest increase in canopy heterogeneity. A Sentinel-2 time series agreed with the UAV data at all three dates (r = 0.71–0.92) and showed the same post-treatment ranking. Single-date comparisons did not reveal these differences. Because the strips were neither randomised nor replicated and no untreated control was included, the differences cannot be attributed unequivocally to the products; a pre-treatment flight and baseline-referenced analysis are nevertheless essential for describing on-farm biostimulant strip trials.

Abstract (英文): Under a warming climate, the impact of alpine permafrost degradation on vegetation dynamics is crucial for understanding ecosystem evolution. Drawing on long-term multi-source observational data from 1986 to 2025 and Structural Equation Modeling (SEM), this study investigated the coupled mechanisms between permafrost degradation and vegetation dynamics in a typical alpine catchment of the Qilian Mountains. The results demonstrated that: (1) Over the past four decades, permafrost in the study area exhibited a continuous degradation pattern, with its areal extent decreasing from 92.5% to 73.8% at a rate of approximately 0.53% yr−1. This degradation was characterized by spatial heterogeneity, showing preferential degradation in the southern region and intensified fragmentation in the central region. (2) The study area experienced an overall significant greening trend. Between 2021 and 2025, areas with high vegetation coverage (Normalized Difference Vegetation Index (NDVI) ≥ 0.09) accounted for 66.4% of the total area, and the greening rate was significantly but only partly modulated by local topography. (3) The permafrost thermal regime exerted a pronounced gradient effect on vegetation dynamics. Vegetation improvement was most rapid in Degraded Permafrost zones, and the promoting effect of active layer deepening on vegetation growth exhibited an optimal threshold of approximately 1.2 m. (4) The freeze-state index showed the strongest statistical association with vegetation dynamics (β = −0.56), whereas the direct precipitation effect was negligible (β = −0.01); the two climate variables co-vary along the elevational gradient and cannot be separated here. Current greening has benefited from hydrothermal conditions released during early permafrost degradation, but this effect may weaken as ground ice is depleted.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dark vessels that deactivate their Automatic Identification System (AIS) or conceal their identities pose substantial challenges to maritime monitoring. A central scientific problem is whether satellite–AIS mismatch can be interpreted as direct evidence of non-cooperative vessel activity, or whether its evidential meaning must be resolved through spatiotemporal AIS support and maritime geographic context. To address this problem, we develop an evidence-driven Dark Vessel Detection Agent that formulates suspected dark-vessel screening as a target-level evidence-interpretation process. The framework integrates Gaofen-6 vessel-like target detection using YOLOv11 oriented bounding boxes, historical AIS-derived navigation context, rule-based geospatial reasoning, DeepSeek-V4-assisted evidence-consistency review, and image-time AIS trajectory rechecking. In the Pearl River Estuary, China, 825 AIS-unexplained targets were reduced to 361 reviewed candidates. Image-time trajectory assessment subsequently identified 114 candidates as AIS-spatiotemporally explainable, while 247 remained unresolved and were retained as prioritized verification tasks, reducing the initial candidate pool by 70.06%. Selective AIS querying reduced query volume by 32.41% relative to exhaustive rechecking and identified 65.14% of the cases meeting the closure criterion. These results show that satellite–AIS mismatch is not a self-sufficient indicator of non-cooperative vessel activity. Its evidential meaning emerges from the joint interpretation of remote-sensing observations, image-time AIS trajectory support, and maritime geographic context.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Climate-risk monitoring requires models that connect satellite observations with natural-language questions while supporting users working in different languages. We present ClimateRS-VL, a parameter-efficient adaptation of Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct for English, Gujarati, and Hindi climate-risk interpretation. The benchmark combines 7182 regional optical–mask pairs from six districts of Gujarat, 1026 nominal regional temporal pairs, 740 reservoir-year metadata records, 409 reservoir-year image samples, and 178,503 programmatically generated multilingual references. A prompt audit confirms that explicit answer-bearing numerical and categorical variables were absent from all 126,810 training, 25,854 validation, and 25,839 test prompts. On the full 25,839-response test evaluation, English, Gujarati, and Hindi VQA obtain BLEU scores of 83.9230, 80.9125, and 83.2524, with BERTScore-F1 values of 0.9891, 0.9961, and 0.9953. A same-backbone study on a common 3000-response subset yields an average VQA BLEU of 0.5625 for zero-shot Qwen2.5-VL, 1.1138 with contextual prompting but no adaptation, and 85.5191 with contextual prompting and LoRA. Question-aligned cross-lingual consistency is 0.9673, although this metric measures agreement rather than correctness. Class-balanced analysis further shows that raw accuracies above 0.98 on four regional risk questions arise from severe low-risk class prevalence: the model predicts the low-risk class for every sample, yielding a balanced accuracy of 0.5000. The protocol is a temporal holdout over recurring monitoring locations rather than an evaluation of unseen-location generalization. The results support parameter-efficient multilingual adaptation for structured in-domain generation while identifying limitations in numerical visual reasoning, minority-risk discrimination, reference diversity, and spatial generalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Soil moisture regulates water and energy exchange between the land surface and the atmosphere, yet most current satellite products have a resolution on the order of tens of kilometers. SoilMERGE (SMERGE) is a 0.125° root zone soil moisture product covering the contiguous United States based on fusion of satellite and land model datasets. Building on earlier tree-based downscaling work, 500 m versions of SMERGE across Kansas, Oklahoma, North Texas, and South Texas were produced. Three comparisons were evaluated: per-state vs. pooled models, land cover as an input variable, and deep learning vs. tree-based models with five algorithms: Yggdrasil Decision Forests random forest (YDFRF) and XGBoost (XGB) as tree-based models. Deep learning models included Gated Linear Units (GLU), TabNet, and a tabular network derived from the Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT). All downscaled products showed improved ranked correlation with AVHRR/VIIRS NDVI compared to the original SMERGE, with the best per-region model raising the ranked correlation coefficient by 0.04 to 0.09 while preserving fidelity to the default SMERGE (anomaly RMSE 0.0136 to 0.0231 m3/m3). In Kansas and Oklahoma, per-state and pooled models performed similarly (mean matched-pixel ΔR −0.016 to +0.007; equivalent within ±0.02 for every model under a spatial block bootstrap). Adding land cover as an input changed the ranked correlation by 0.0053 on average in absolute value. Evaluated through agreement with vegetation response, these results support further development of a state-based 500 m version of SMERGE in the Southern Great Plains.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of gross primary productivity (GPP) is essential for monitoring terrestrial photosynthesis and ecosystem responses to environmental change. We synthesized 81 studies retrieved from the Web of Science to explore reported performance patterns of spectral reflectance- and solar-induced chlorophyll fluorescence (SIF)-based GPP estimation. Approaches were classified by the GPP predictors and their provenance, distinguishing reflectance-based estimates from those based on observation-retrieved SIF. Mean study-level R2 values were 0.691 for reflectance-based approaches and 0.661 for SIF-based approaches, with no significant difference between them (p = 0.384). Both approaches exhibited substantial between-study heterogeneity and distinct temporal responses. Subgroup analyses examined vegetation type, environmental stress, and temporal resolution. SIF-based pooled R2 was lower under stress than under non-stress conditions (0.525 versus 0.735; p = 0.020). Reflectance-based pooled R2 also decreased numerically, from 0.719 to 0.625, but the difference was not significant (p = 0.499). Reflectance-based pooled R2 was highest for C4 crops, whereas differences among vegetation types were not significant for SIF-based approaches. Weekly SIF-based estimates had a higher pooled R2 than subdaily estimates (0.736 versus 0.652; p = 0.023). Incorporating canopy structure and soil water content was associated with the largest reported mean relative improvements in R2 for reflectance- and SIF-based approaches, respectively. These findings support selecting GPP estimation approaches according to vegetation type, environmental conditions, and temporal scale, and combining reflectance and SIF with relevant structural and environmental information.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rivers deliver particulate organic carbon (POC) to the coastal ocean together with suspended sediment, yet it remains unclear whether the seasonal cycle of surface POC follows that of the suspended-particle field. We combined monthly Sentinel-3 Ocean and Land Colour Instrument (OLCI) POC for 2022–2025, hourly Geostationary Ocean Color Imager-II (GOCI-II) observations, and field data in the Changjiang Estuary and the adjacent East China Sea. The OLCI POC retrieval yielded a root-mean-square error of 0.288 dex in log10 POC against 96 station-scene matchups, and a residual-fusion algorithm transferred the OLCI concentration level to hourly GOCI-II observations. Retrieved POC peaked in summer near the estuary and in winter farther seaward. These two annual cycles were nearly opposite, and their equal-weight midpoint lay approximately 46 km seaward of the maximum logarithmic gradient of total suspended solids (TSS) (95% year-resampling interval, 40–53 km). Between the two regimes, the annual amplitude reached its minimum, and morning–afternoon differences in the fused fields were comparable to the annual amplitude. Near the estuary, field POC was higher in summer, when TSS was lower, identifying the summer maximum as a carbon signal. Over the outer shelf, field POC showed no cold-season increase despite higher TSS, indicating that the winter maximum includes an optical effect of particle composition. Field POC increased sublinearly with TSS, such that the carbon content of suspended particles decreased with increasing TSS.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tropical and subtropical forests harbor over half of the world’s terrestrial biodiversity and account for a large fraction of the global terrestrial carbon sink. However, under accelerating climate change and human pressure, the dynamics of these forests remain incompletely characterized, particularly in rapidly developing regions where multiple change processes—fire, clear-cutting, afforestation, and deforestation—operate simultaneously. Using 30 m Landsat time-series imagery spanning 1993–2021, fused with China’s annual land-cover dataset (CLCD) and the continuous change-detection algorithm (CCDC), this study constructed a unified, multi-type forest-change dataset for tropical and subtropical China. The spatiotemporal patterns of four dominant change types were quantified through grid-based intensity analysis and the Mann–Kendall trend test, and associations with annual intensity classes were examined using an eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) ensemble learner combined with SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) model interpretation. Our results reveal the following: First, spatially, fire and clear-cutting both exhibit a southeast–southwest dual-core aggregation pattern, while afforestation and deforestation show highly congruent, largely dispersed distributions dominated by the moderate period-summary intensity class. Second, temporally, fire shows an overall declining trend, with 52.1% of grid cells exhibiting significant or non-significant decreases; clear-cutting shows significant increases in 28.4% of grid cells. Afforestation and deforestation are both dominated by significant and non-significant increases: 48.7% and 62.8% of grid cells, respectively. For deforestation, 44.8% of the grids included in the peak-year analysis reached their maximum annual intensity during 2011–2015, although this concentration may partly reflect temporal inconsistencies in the underlying CLCD data. Third, the annual intensity-class models identify potential evapotranspiration as a leading predictor for fire and mean temperature as an important environmental covariate for clear-cutting. Slope and population are important predictors for both afforestation and deforestation. In the spatial summaries of SHAP contributions to the annual high-intensity class output, slope is a prominent negative contributor, particularly for afforestation and deforestation. This study provides a unified, multi-type, multi-decade analysis of forest change in tropical and subtropical China, characterizes nonlinear associations with annual intensity-class outputs, and maps their dominant positive and negative SHAP contributors to support precision forest management and climate-adaptive ecological policy design.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Road incidents involving public-transport vehicles and hazardous-material carriers require ground-based RGB sensing capable of localizing full vehicle extent while identifying visible fire cues under severe background clutter, scale variation, and illumination artifacts. To address the tension between preserving macro-level vehicle semantics and capturing localized fire patterns, we propose the Robust Dual-Path Fusion Detector (RDF-Det), centered on a parallel feature-fusion neck (RDF-Neck) that integrates top–down semantic propagation with bidirectional spatial aggregation. By decoupling the dual-path topology from branch-mixing parameterization, we systematically investigate four routing strategies—level-wise learned, globally shared, fixed-uniform, and parameter-matched frozen routing—and evaluate cross-family instantiations across RTMDet, YOLOX, and Faster R-CNN. Across a 100-run five-seed benchmark, the main RDF-Det configuration (RTM-RDF-GLR-DINO) achieves mean validation scores of 0.6352 AP and 0.9290 AP50, delivering a system-level improvement of 7.24 AP points over RTM-Base and outperforming the YOLO26 detector (0.6235 AP and 0.9071 AP50). Controlled evaluations on both the validation split and a screened test subset demonstrate that lightweight global and fixed-uniform fusion remain highly competitive with complex level-wise routing, while cross-family comparisons clarify the architectural conditions governing one-stage and two-stage adaptation. Supported by repeated runs, confidence intervals, and Holm-corrected paired tests, this study provides both an effective vehicle-fire detection framework and systematic empirical insights into dual-path multi-scale fusion.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hourly quantitative precipitation estimation (QPE) from weather radar remains difficult during intense rainfall because fixed empirical relations and temporal averaging may not represent rapid scan-to-scan changes and localized echo structure. We evaluated five convolutional neural network configurations using S-band dual-polarization radar and rain-gauge observations in Guangzhou, China. A locally calibrated composite dual-polarization estimator (Composite-DP) provided a conventional reference. Each sample contains ZH, ZDR, and KDP from ten consecutive 0.5° scans over the preceding hour in a 3 × 10 × 21 × 21 tensor. Three-dimensional convolution spans time and two horizontal dimensions, without vertical-volume input. The dataset from 2018 to 2021 comprised 3,008,738 matched samples with rainfall ≥ 0.1 mm h−1, including 2,611,457 for training, 149,221 from six 2020 events for validation, and 141,428 from six 2021 events for testing. The models included a temporally averaged 2D baseline (2DPoly-QPENet), a time-resolved 3D baseline (3DPoly-QPENet), separate temporal (3DTime-QPENet) and multiscale (3DEcho-QPENet) extensions, and their fusion (3DHybrid-QPENet). The temporal extension represented the encoded ten-step sequence with a convolutional long short-term memory (ConvLSTM) layer, whereas the multiscale extension used an encoder–decoder pathway. On the independent test set, Composite-DP yielded an RMSE of 4.139 mm h−1. Compared with 2DPoly-QPENet, 3DPoly-QPENet had a lower mean absolute error (MAE; 1.321 versus 1.524 mm h−1) and root-mean-square error (RMSE; 3.088 versus 3.307 mm h−1), while its correlation coefficient (CC) was higher (0.860 versus 0.832). Among the individual extensions, 3DEcho-QPENet had lower overall errors than 3DTime-QPENet. Their fusion, 3DHybrid-QPENet, achieved the lowest overall MAE (1.270 mm h−1) and RMSE (2.971 mm h−1), with a Bias of −0.537 mm h−1 and the highest CC (0.866). Across the baseline and extension comparisons, the time-resolved 3D representation and the joint temporal and multiscale configuration were associated with lower hourly QPE errors. 3DHybrid-QPENet also had the lowest relative error (RE), MAE, and RMSE in all five rainfall-intensity classes. Above 50 mm h−1, however, its Bias remained −20.031 mm h−1, indicating persistent underestimation. Generalization beyond the study region remains to be evaluated.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper presents a knowledge-grounded Digital Twin framework designed to support the interpretation of long-term structural monitoring data. The approach combines AI reasoning models, project-specific knowledge retrieval, numerical observations, and spatial information within an interactive environment where specialists can explore monitoring data and formulate analytical requests. The framework is demonstrated through the Milan Cathedral case study, using ground-based measurements, satellite interferometry data, and a selected subset of technical reports covering more than two decades. A Cesium-based 3D environment, including gaussian splatting visualization, provides spatial access to the monitoring information. The application illustrates how specialists can use AI to retrieve relevant knowledge, guide computational analyses, and examine relationships between monitoring observations and previous engineering interpretations. New forms of interaction among specialists, AI, and monitoring information are illustrated and discussed, highlighting the complementary roles of accumulated knowledge, AI-supported analysis, and human expertise in the interpretation process.

Abstract (英文): Recovering 3D structure from multi-view satellite images is fundamental to urban planning, disaster assessment, and land monitoring. 3D Gaussian splatting (3DGS) offers an explicit geometric representation and real-time rendering, making it a promising alternative to stereo matching and neural radiance fields for satellite photogrammetry. Existing satellite 3DGS methods couple geometric optimization tightly with photometric signals. This coupling often leads to incomplete and overly smooth surfaces in vegetation and weak-texture regions. In this paper, we propose elevation-consistent 3D Gaussian splatting (ECGS), a method that breaks this coupling at the level of primitive creation and removal through three modules. First, we propose a multi-view elevation consistency regularization to establish cross-view geometric anchors, addressing elevation ambiguity without relying on photometric consistency. Second, a decoupled structure-aware densification mechanism is designed with an independent geometric pathway that keeps adding primitives where photometric gradients are unreliable. Finally, we introduce an importance-guided pruning strategy that removes the exposed redundancy through importance-weighted scores, while a geometric safety lock protects structural details. Experiments on the DFC2019 and IARPA 2016 benchmarks show that ECGS achieves better elevation estimates and higher-quality novel views. By jointly exploiting the radiometric appearance of multi-view imagery and the geometric evidence of cross-view correspondences, ECGS demonstrates that fusing these two modalities is an effective path toward intelligent remote sensing interpretation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite video supports continuous vehicle monitoring, but processing delays can leave detections misaligned with current target positions. In tiled inference, release scheduling determines when regional detections become available, while overlap fusion combines them into a full-frame output. We develop a timeliness evaluation framework that matches available full-frame detections against query-time targets and tracks tile source frames and result availability. With detector weights fixed within each comparison, we evaluate release frequency, fusion, and construction cost through controlled replay and actual online execution. Fixed-lag experiments show that query-time quality generally declines as lag increases on VISO, SAT-MTB, and SDM-Car. Common-clock replay on SDM-Car shows that earlier regional release improves query-time matching with identical end-of-cycle detections. In online SDM-Car experiments with full-candidate fusion, batch-wise release at baseline load without motion prediction increased multi-frame DSFNet query-time F1 by 8.75% relative to end-of-cycle release. DSFNet benefited with causal motion prediction enabled; YOLOX-S benefited under increased processing load. Comparisons across fusion strategies and measured construction costs further showed that release gains depended on candidate retention and operating conditions. These findings support jointly selecting release frequency and overlap fusion while accounting for output construction cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Time-series interferometric synthetic aperture radar (InSAR) reveals spatially heterogeneous slope deformation, but a slope-wide statistic alone cannot describe the contribution of spatially clustered pixels with pronounced rates. Both statistics were calculated for 116 adjacent-acquisition intervals from 117 ascending Sentinel-1A images of three Xiaowan Reservoir slopes. A five-class interpretation identified concurrent and divergent changes; transitional states accounted for 57–61 of the 115 intervals assigned to operational periods at each slope. During 22 April–4 May 2024, all three slopes reached their annual ID maxima. In the following interval, ID fell sharply while LSC remained relatively high. At Anlecun, the lower-tail contribution to LSC fell from 0.02548 to 0.00361 mm d−1, whereas the upper-tail contribution rose from 0.01298 to 0.03973 mm d−1. Thus, a high total LSC accompanied a substantial change in its tail composition. Pooled annual peak maps showed differences in clustered-pixel locations between selected peak and following intervals, providing spatial information that the LSC value alone does not retain. Together, the paired series, tail contributions, and maps can guide closer examination of intervals in which slope-wide activity and clustered-tail contributions differ. Inclinometers at Kongquedu provided limited directional consistency for stage-level changes.

Abstract (英文): Ground-penetrating radar (GPR) inversion using deep learning may experience substantial performance degradation when acquisition parameters differ between training and testing. One important factor contributing to this sensitivity is the conventional use of raw time-domain representations, in which information associated with different frequency bands, propagation scales, and phase characteristics is implicitly mixed. To address this problem, we propose a token-enhanced Transformer–mixture-of-experts (MoE) U-Net (TET-MoE), a physics-aware representation-learning framework that integrates three complementary components: multiscale wavelet-plus-phase tokenization for structured signal representation, lightweight Transformer blocks for global cross-scale dependency modeling, and a convolutional MoE module with sparse dynamic routing for adaptive feature processing at the bottleneck. The pretraining–encoder-freezing–decoder-fine-tuning strategy adapts the model to 200 MHz, a target frequency omitted from pretraining and within the 80–300 MHz span of the six source frequencies. The 200 MHz fine-tuning inputs amount to less than 1% of the source-domain training inputs. TET-MoE outperforms the baseline U-Net under matched fine-tuning conditions at 200 MHz. Ablation studies, noise tests, and auxiliary classification further evaluate the model. For field inference, the source-pretrained model was fine-tuned on synthetic 110 MHz data and then applied to field data collected with a nominal 100 MHz antenna. The results indicate that the proposed design improves inversion accuracy, convergence behavior, and structural fidelity under the evaluated conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Road extraction from remote sensing imagery is a structured problem: a mask with high region overlap may still contain breaks that make the road network unusable, and the difficulty grows when annotated sources are small, heterogeneous, and unequally represented during joint training. We revisit this problem on a SAM2 encoder while keeping all added complexity in training rather than in the deployed network. First, we do not use SAM2 as a black box: its top-down pathway refines only the coarsest levels, leaving the quarter-resolution features our decoder needs semantically shallow, so a feature-enhancement neck restores the missing top-down path and a directional attention module matches the elongated geometry of roads. Second, we decompose road structure into five complementary geometric views—skeleton, direction, width, neighbourhood affinity, and endpoints—used as a shared training-time prior; each is a deterministic function of the segmentation mask, so the prior needs no extra annotation and no learned generator, and its heads are removed after training, leaving the inference graph free of auxiliary computation; the feature-enhancement neck and directional attention module remain in the deployed model, as they serve the segmentation head. A soft-clDice term supervises centrelines, and a square-root sampler rebalances the 5.2:1 update-frequency skew across sources. On Massachusetts, DeepGlobe, and CHN6-CUG, the model reaches an aggregate IoU of 0.7403 and CC-agreement of 0.6874, improving CC-agreement by 44% over D-LinkNet and 10% over the strongest same-backbone adaptation. We also make evaluation part of the contribution: we measure an empirical uncertainty scale for topology metrics and hold every claim to exceed it. Held to this standard, the prior’s CC-agreement gain clears the scale only in the scarce-annotation regime that limits operational mapping yet costs nothing to retain elsewhere. Finally, region overlap and CC-agreement rank models differently, so both metric families should be reported against a stated measurement scale.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Local displacement, illumination changes, and repeated roof textures can produce false building changes in bi-temporal remote-sensing images. We propose PINF-RCAF to detect change from the discrepancy that remains after local transport and nuisance regression. The Pseudo-Invariant Nuisance Field (PINF) estimates displacement-penalized bidirectional transport, and Pseudo-Invariant Residual Regression (PIRR) removes the locally explained component. Reciprocal Correlation Alignment Fusion (RCAF) combines local correlation, forward–backward cycle agreement, and learned trust to control semantic and boundary corrections. A direct residual path to the decoder preserves evidence for structures without a reliable temporal counterpart. At a fixed threshold of 0.50, three independently seeded training runs yield mean test F1/IoU of 0.9305/0.8701 on FOTBCD, 0.9448/0.8954 on LEVIR-CD, and 0.9722/0.9460 on DSIFN-CD. The mean F1 gains over the matched Control are 3.49, 2.58, and 1.26 percentage points, respectively. Component comparisons identify the gains from local matching and residual correction. Boundary evaluation and controlled validation-set appearance perturbations further show improved contour placement and fewer background responses. These results support the use of correspondence reliability to refine residual change evidence.

Abstract (英文): Infrared small target detection (IRSTD) requires the preservation of extremely weak target responses while suppressing structured background clutter. Existing detectors have substantially improved feature representation, yet two issues remain common across architectures: shallow features are usually processed without explicitly measuring local statistical distinctiveness, and high-resolution details are often fused uniformly without considering the reliability of the current prediction. To address these issues, we propose LSUR, a lightweight plug-and-play refinement framework composed of multi-scale local statistical enhancement (MLSE) and confidence–uncertainty-guided refinement (CUGR). MLSE measures the standardized deviation of shallow feature responses within multiple local neighborhoods and adaptively integrates the resulting statistical responses, thereby enhancing locally distinctive target cues with varying background contrast. CUGR derives target-confidence and uncertainty maps from the base prediction and uses them to selectively retrieve high-resolution information for residual logit refinement. The framework requires only one shallow feature, the prediction feature, and the base output, allowing it to be attached to different encoder–decoder IRSTD detectors. Extensive experiments on three public IRSTD datasets and multiple representative detectors demonstrate that LSUR consistently improves detection performance with limited additional computational cost. Specifically, integrating LSUR with SCTransNet improves IoU by 1.68, 0.73, and 1.44 percentage points on NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K, respectively. Ablation studies further confirm the effectiveness of local statistical modeling, multi-scale aggregation, and confidence–uncertainty-guided refinement.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate highway region segmentation is essential for road inspection and traffic scene understanding. However, complex backgrounds, viewpoint variations, occlusions and shadows often lead to incomplete segmentation of road regions and inaccurate boundary predictions. This paper proposes an improved UNet model, DS-UNet, for fine-grained highway region segmentation from UAV imagery. A Dilated-Global Context Block (DGCBlock) is embedded within the encoder to fuse multi-scale dilated features with global context information, enhancing the feature representation of continuous road structures. Meanwhile, a Semantic-Guided Channel-Spatial Attention (SGCSA) module is devised for the decoder, in which high-level semantic features guide channel-spatial feature recalibration to boost fine-detail reconstruction and suppress background interference. In addition, a boundary-weighted BCE-Dice hybrid loss (BWD) is proposed to strengthen supervision for road boundary pixels. To evaluate the model’s performance, this paper constructed the UAVRR3K dataset consisting of 3000 highway region images captured under diverse UAV perspectives and complex road scenarios. Experimental results show that DS-UNet achieves an F1 of 97.31% and a Road-class IoU of 94.76%, outperforming comparative models. Benefiting from the proposed BWD loss, boundary recall reaches 77.88%. These results verify that our method effectively enhances road region integrity and boundary completeness, offering reliable technical support for UAV-based intelligent highway inspection.

Abstract (英文): Aerial imagery objects present characteristics such as arbitrary orientations, dense distribution, and a wide range of scales. Mainstream detectors add angles to traditional rectangular bounding boxes to better fit the object boundaries, but this makes the detection task even more challenging. Non-Maximum Suppression (NMS) is a filter designed to address duplicate detections in dense detectors, extracting the most representative output from multiple detection results for a single object. However, the NMS algorithm has fundamental problems, including the risk of wrong suppression due to confidence-based prioritization, poor computational speed from sequential processing, and inefficient use of its output data. To address the above challenges, and based on the data patterns of objects in remote sensing imagery, we reviewed NMS as a local density peak clustering task and proposed a novel post-processing method called DPNMS. We integrated information across each output through statistical analysis to construct its density representations, and then searched for peak values within valid neighborhoods defined by the IoU threshold. To prevent suppressing effective boxes, we designed a “select–release” cyclic workflow to correct the chosen result. The proposed method integrates the detector’s output information to achieve competitive results. Our method does not require sorting the localization information in reverse order of confidence, and each operation is independent of the others, allowing for parallelization and resulting in high computational efficiency. Extensive experiments conducted on various datasets and multiple platforms have validated the effectiveness and stability of the proposed method. The unique filtering strategy offers a new framework for post-processing methods in remote sensing image object detection.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite assessments of vegetation responses to tropical cyclones depend on both indicator sensitivity and observational availability. We evaluated 21 storm-country study areas in Southern Africa from 2018 to 2021 using TROPOspheric Monitoring Instrument (TROPOMI) solar-induced chlorophyll fluorescence (SIF) as a photosynthesis-linked indicator and Aqua Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS) normalised difference vegetation index (NDVI) as an independent measure of canopy greenness. Storm-week responses within 200 km of cyclone tracks were estimated using spatial-block bootstrap intervals. Usable storm-week SIF was available for 12 study areas (nested within seven cyclones), whereas NDVI was available for all 21. Across the 12 matched study areas, SIF changes ranged from −42.9% to +35.5% and NDVI changes from −4.0% to +6.1%. The +35.5% Desmond extreme represents storm-week SIF remaining above the pre-storm baseline while a pre-existing greening trajectory was interrupted, not cyclone-enhanced photosynthesis; the −42.9% Chalane–Zimbabwe extreme is based on two usable SIF days and is interpreted cautiously. At the study-area level, the indicators agreed in two cases, opposed one another in three, and were statistically unresolved in seven because at least one interval included zero; these 2/3/7 counts do not represent independent cyclone events. Diane, Francisco, and Kenneth showed SIF decreases alongside NDVI increases, demonstrating that greenness did not reproduce the direction of the photosynthesis-linked response. NDVI generally returned to its seasonal range earlier than SIF. These findings show that SIF and NDVI provide complementary but non-equivalent evidence and that sparse SIF sampling determines which cyclone responses can be distinguished from no change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Following the launch of the Surface Water Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission, data collected during the calibration/validation “fast sampling” phase were acquired daily for select swaths, presenting a unique opportunity to monitor ice break-up for Lhù’ààn Mǟn (Kluane Lake, Yukon Territory, Canada) using the Ka-band Radar Interferometer (KaRIn). The KaRIn pixel cloud product is designed to provide backscatter and surface elevation for open water; however, it effectively discriminates ice-covered regions on the lake. In this study, the efficacy of the KaRIn pixel cloud product is evaluated for the retrieval of the timing of water-clear-of-ice (WCI), an ice phenology parameter that has proven difficult to resolve using co- or cross-polarized synthetic aperture radar. The Dynamic Threshold Variability Method (DTVM) is evaluated against optical remote sensing from near-daily PlanetScope acquisitions, and is also within the physically plausible range of lake-wide melt out dates simulated by the one-dimensional thermodynamic Canadian Lake Ice Model (CLIMo). At 11 Points of Interest (POIs), SWOT observed the transition from break-up to water-clear-of-ice phase from 23 May 2023 to 30 May 2023, a total of 8 days, with an RMSE of 1.2 days (95% confidence interval of 0.6–1.7 days), and a Mean Absolute Error (MAE) of 0.8 days (95% confidence interval of 0.4–1 days) for WCI derived using the Dynamic Threshold Variability Method (DTVM) at a tessellated resolution of 100 m. When comparing PlanetScope WCI to the DTVM using the 50th percentile of thresholds for all 100 m tessellation pixels across the lake, the RMSE is 8.69 days, with an MAE of 3.15 days. While SWOT has since transitioned to the science phase (21-day repeat pass), the use of KaRIn during the fast sampling phase to retrieve WCI presents the opportunity to discriminate between melting freshwater ice and open water during the break-up season that previous SAR observations have had difficulty resolving.

</details>

While differences in acquisition geometry precluded point-wise sensor equivalence, both modalities effectively discriminated inoculated from non-inoculated leaves within each plant using four common spectral bands via spatially grouped cross-validation (balanced accuracy: 0.923–0.930 for ‘Canasta’; 0.824–0.866 for ‘Romana’). Furthermore, full 204-band HSI models yielded improved tissue discrimination. The results highlight the need to distinguish internal spectral consistency, local cross-sensor correspondence, and task-level information when evaluating heterogeneous spectral–3D observations for future joint use.

Abstract (英文): The objective of this paper is to describe a robust technique to perform the inter-satellite radiometric calibration (XCAL) between two microwave radiometers of significantly different designs. Because this results in the relative calibration between the two, one is the reference and the other is the to-be-calibrated target. This technique makes no judgment as to which is the best absolute calibration; rather, it only considers the relative difference between the two. For our application, the Global Precipitation Measurement Microwave Imager (GMI) is the transfer standard for the inter-satellite radiometric calibration of a constellation of cooperative satellites’ microwave radiometers used to provide global precipitation measurements for an international community of scientific and operational users. This paper describes the on-orbit validation process for a future candidate GPM constellation member, namely, the 91.6-GHz TROPICS Pathfinder radiometer. A one-year TROPICS–GMI matchup dataset was developed using spatially and temporally collocated brightness temperature observations over clear-sky ocean scenes. Also, these brightness temperature data were combined with estimates of the associated environmental parameters, provided by a global numerical weather model product. The associated XCAL analysis procedures are described, and the radiometric bias results are presented.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based multi-object tracking (MOT) is important for intelligent traffic analysis but remains challenging because small object scales, frequent occlusions, motion blur, and changing aerial viewpoints jointly degrade detection quality and trajectory continuity. This paper presents HiMoTrack, a tracking-by-detection framework that addresses this degradation chain by improving small-object observations and progressively recovering low-quality tracks. Specifically, a Small Object Feature Efficient Perception Network (SOFEPNet) preserves spatial detail and reconstructs multi-scale features for aerial small objects. For motion recovery, a Velocity Adaptive Cubature Kalman Filter (VACKF) provides reliability-adaptive short-term state estimation, while a Group Motion Compensation Strategy (GMCS) exploits local group-motion consistency when individual observations become unreliable. When no reliable neighboring motion prior is available, Spatio-Temporal Memory Prediction (STMP) uses historical trajectory memory to infer the missing state and maintain temporal continuity. In this way, the detector, motion estimator, and trajectory recovery process are organized around the same objective: stabilizing weak and interrupted trajectories in complex UAV traffic scenes. Experiments show that HiMoTrack achieves multi-object tracking accuracy (MOTA) scores of 64.6% on the UAVDT benchmark and 62.4% on VisDrone while maintaining strong overall tracking accuracy and competitive trajectory continuity. Additional experiments on MOT17 demonstrate that the proposed motion recovery strategy can be integrated into representative trackers, indicating good modularity and transferability.

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image–text retrieval requires diverse textual supervision and fine-grained visual representations. Redundant captions limit linguistic diversity, while rewriting can introduce semantic drift. A single global image descriptor may also underrepresent small objects and local spatial arrangements, limiting fine-grained discrimination. To address these challenges, we propose RS-DeCLIP, a two-stage retrieval framework. Stage 1 adapts a RemoteCLIP ViT-B/32 dual encoder with DeCLIP-style multi-view supervision using official captions and captions polished by a large language model. A semantic consistency gate downweights text self-supervision (TSS) between less consistent original and rewritten captions. Similarity-aware negative downweighting and text-to-image weighting address ambiguous negatives and directional imbalance, while training-only fine-grained alignment strengthens detail sensitivity. Stage 2 targets the fine-grained visual information underrepresented by the global descriptor. Five learnable visual query vectors, conditioned on the global image embedding and guided by soft spatial preferences, attend to a dense P2 feature map to extract local evidence. Consistency-gated orthogonal residual fusion integrates this evidence into the frozen global image embedding. These visual queries are distinct from the user’s text query: image feature extraction and fusion do not use the retrieval text, so fused image descriptors can be precomputed and compared with independently encoded text vectors. On the Remote Sensing Image Captioning Dataset (RSICD), Stage 1 improves mean recall (MR) from 35.26% to 38.54%, and Stage 2 further increases MR to 39.27%. On the Remote Sensing Image–Text Match Dataset (RSITMD), Stage 2 improves MR from 50.90% to 52.58%. The final model exceeds the reported GeoRSCLIP-FT (RET-2) results by 0.40 and 0.77 percentage points on RSICD and RSITMD, respectively, and the Representation Discrepancy Bridging (RDB) method by 1.18 and 0.54 percentage points.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Regional winter rapeseed yield prediction requires models that remain reliable across highly variable climate conditions. We developed an interpretable climate-responsive ensemble for rapeseed yield prediction using satellite and environmental predictors. We combined their predictions using regime-specific weights. The ensemble achieved a coefficient of determination (R2) of 0.848, a root mean square error (RMSE) of 189 kg ha−1, a mean absolute error (MAE) of 151 kg ha−1 and a relative RMSE of 8.9%. Abstract (英文): Mining-induced subsidence is a common geological hazard in coal mining areas, and reliable deformation monitoring is important for risk reduction. Cumulative InSAR deformation fields contain deformation accumulated during preceding periods, whereas newly occurring deformation over a short forecast horizon generally represents only a small fraction of the cumulative magnitude. Direct prediction of future cumulative deformation may therefore exploit temporal persistence to achieve relatively low mean errors while underestimating locally strong subsidence. To investigate this issue, we used 276 ascending Sentinel-1 SAR images acquired from 10 January 2020 to 11 July 2024 to perform DS-InSAR monitoring at the Wutongzhuang Mine, Hebei Province, China, and constructed 138 continuous two-dimensional cumulative vertical deformation fields. We defined three prediction tasks—cumulative-input–cumulative-output (C2C), cumulative-input–increment-output (C2D), and increment-input–increment-output (D2D)—and applied each task to CNN-PhLSTM, ConvLSTM, and VMRNN. Monitoring identified two major subsidence zones in the north-central and southwestern parts of the study area, with a maximum cumulative vertical deformation of approximately −467.96mm by 11 July 2024. The Pearson correlation of colocated PS-InSAR and DS-InSAR deformation rates was 0.88; comparison between DS-InSAR and leveling at P1 and P2 yielded a correlation of 0.9742, a mean absolute error (MAE) of 8.23 mm, and a root mean square error (RMSE) of 10.29 mm. Keeping historical cumulative deformation inputs unchanged and converting the future supervision target to deformation increments reduced the RMSEs of CNN-PhLSTM, ConvLSTM, and VMRNN by 56.48%, 12.17%, and 17.16%, respectively, with consistent improvement directions across three random seeds. Further converting historical inputs to increments produced smaller, model-dependent changes. Under the unified D2D task, VMRNN achieved the lowest mean RMSE and MAE and the highest mean R2, showing relatively favorable overall predictive performance among the compared models. Spatial predictions also show that VMRNN-D2D generally preserves the spatial structures of the major subsidence zones and local troughs, while errors are concentrated mainly in actively developing strong-subsidence areas, where subsidence magnitudes tend to be underestimated. These findings indicate that representing the future supervision target as deformation increments provides an effective strategy for improving short-term prediction of cumulative InSAR deformation across different networks.

Abstract (英文): An X5.8-class solar flare began at 01:10 UT on 11 May 2024, during the main phase of the super geomagnetic storm, providing an opportunity to investigate solar flare effects under highly disturbed space weather conditions. Using multi-instrument observations from the Sanya incoherent scatter radar (ISR), ionosondes, Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) receivers, and ground magnetometers, this study investigates the ionospheric and geomagnetic responses over East Asia. The Sanya ISR observations revealed pronounced electron density enhancements near 110 km and at 300–400 km, with electron density at 300–400 km reaching 1.92–4.08 times the background values. The F2-layer peak electron density (NmF2) increased rapidly to 2.02 × 1012 el/m3, approximately three times its preflare value, whereas the F2-layer peak height (hmF2) initially decreased and subsequently increased. The ISR-derived vertical total electron content (TEC) increased by approximately 20 TECU relative to the reference day, with the fractional contribution from around 350 km increasing by approximately 5 percentage points. Ionosonde observations revealed pronounced latitudinal differences. F-region echoes at Mohe and Beijing nearly disappeared against the background of a strong negative ionospheric storm, whereas the flare-associated response was more prominent at the low-latitude station Sanya, where the minimum reflection frequency (fmin) increased to 8.85–9.95 MHz. GNSS-TEC observations revealed distinct latitudinal differences in the ionospheric response, with maximum TEC enhancements of 36.76 and 35.40 TECU at the low-latitude stations SYBG and QION, respectively. At middle latitudes, the negative ionospheric storm remained dominant despite short-term positive flare-associated enhancements, while some stations in the northeastern high-latitude region exhibited small positive perturbations superimposed on the negative background. The geomagnetic response exhibited a pronounced east–west asymmetry within the 40–50°N latitude band, with positive disturbances in the horizontal component of the geomagnetic field over 75–95°E and negative disturbances over 96–135°E. These findings suggest that flare-induced photoionization and conductivity enhancement interacted with the pre-existing storm-time electrodynamic environment, producing altitude-dependent ionospheric responses and pronounced spatial variations in TEC and geomagnetic disturbances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Arid ecosystems face severe climate threats, yet stochastic climate volatility often masks true ecological transitions. In this study, we developed an integrated spatiotemporal framework to decouple mean ecological trends from climate noise in Gansu Province, China. We combined Remote Sensing Ecological Index (RSEI) time series, the multi-scalar Standardized Precipitation Evapotranspiration Index (SPEI), and a probabilistic Dust Hazard Index (DHI) to evaluate historical dynamics (2001–2024). Subsequently, eco-environmental sustainability (2025–2099) was projected under CMIP6 scenarios using a Random Forest algorithm optimized via spatial block cross-validation. Historical evaluations revealed a significant regional ecological amelioration (RSEI trend: +0.0279/decade, p < 0.01), with the degraded area decreasing from 68.60% in 2001 to 52.68% in 2024. However, spatial polarization persisted, with severe degradation covering 38.84% of the hyper-arid northwest. Feature importance and dual correlation analyses identified short-term moisture deficits (SPEI-3) and chronic dust exposure as primary biophysical controls; notably, dust hazards strongly suppressed vegetation in degraded zones (r = −0.43). Future projections indicate that warming-induced aridity will intensify ecological stress. Under the intermediate SSP2-4.5 scenario, the area of Serious Decline (SSD) expands from 35.09% in the near term to 47.15% by the end of the century. Conversely, the high-emission SSP5-8.5 scenario, characterized by thermal extremes up to 3.58 °C, triggers an abrupt structural transition during 2061–2099; the SSD area more than triples (from 12.13% to 43.95%), overwhelming historical restoration gains. Safeguarding these fragile drylands necessitates proactive management integrating targeted dust mitigation and water rights allocation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visible–infrared fusion is a main route to all-weather object extraction in near-ground remote sensing, but single-band RGB remains unstable under occlusion, clutter, and low light and illumination variation. This study presents LightMaskMamba, a lightweight visible–infrared object detection network for near-ground remote sensing. Public benchmarks test fusion quality across scenes, and the self-built near-ground wampee field dataset is for deployment-oriented field validation. The core of the network is a lightweight mask-guided Mamba fusion architecture. Its key module, the Lightweight Mask Mamba Fusion Block (LMMFB), uses an object-related mask to drive shallow-to-deep VIS–NIR feature fusion, focuses computation on object regions, and reduces scan redundancy. Dual-band detection therefore stays light enough to run at the edge. On the self-built wampee dataset, LightMaskMamba-v11s reaches 97.4% mAP50 and 52.0% mAP50:95. Relative to single-band YOLOv11s, mAP50 is 2.3 and 4.6 percentage points higher on IR and RGB. Parameters rise by 3.7M, from 8.8M to 12.5M. The same model is likewise effective in detection accuracy, and lighter, on public multimodal object-detection datasets. On a Jetson Orin NX Super, TensorRT INT8 mixed-precision inference reaches about 17.3 FPS, or 58 ms per image.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Seafloor sediment classification is critical for benthic habitat mapping and marine environmental protection. Existing classification methods mostly adopt seafloor surface observation parameters such as backscatter and bathymetry. These parameters only characterize surface seabed properties and lack physical prior information related to sediment formation mechanisms, which limits classification accuracy and physical interpretability. Considering that bottom-current kinetic energy (BCKE) governs sediment erosion, transport and deposition and further shapes the spatial distribution of sediments, this paper proposes a bottom-current-fused two-stage Bayesian MIC-augmented mRMR (BCTBM) architecture for seafloor sediment classification. BCTBM combines BCKE with acoustic and topographic features to construct a 77-dimensional multi-source feature set. Samples with high class separability are selected using the Jeffries–Matusita (J-M) distance, and a two-stage feature selection algorithm is further developed. ReliefF is first applied for coarse-grained feature screening to generate a candidate feature set; subsequently, the Bayesian MIC-augmented Minimum Redundancy Maximum Relevance (BM-mRMR) model employs Bayesian optimization (BO) to adaptively solve the optimal relevance–redundancy trade-off weights for seven target dimensions, and mRMR finally derives the optimal feature set based on the obtained optimal weights. The proposed method is validated in the shallow-water sediment classification scenario of the eastern English Channel. Eighty-four comparative experiments demonstrate that introducing BCKE improves the overall accuracy by 3.48% on average, with a maximum increment of 9.4%. The proposed two-stage BM-mRMR method achieves average accuracy improvements of 4.84% and 6.08% over ReliefF and ReliefF-mRMR, respectively. The classification results are consistent with the Hjulström–Sundborg sedimentary-dynamic framework, offering an effective pathway for refined benthic habitat mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-definition (HD) maps for autonomous driving store each lane marking as a separate polyline. Satellite imagery covers wide areas at low cost, but current methods cannot turn it directly into such polylines. The reason is that no existing output representation suits polyline detection. Segmentation masks do not tell which line a pixel belongs to, and they merge lines that meet or cross. Existing cell-wise connectivity representations tie their output slots to fixed angular sectors. Their training target therefore changes discontinuously at sector boundaries. We propose STELLA, an output representation suited to polylines, and a method to train it. At each grid cell, STELLA predicts one self-slot and two unordered connection slots. During training, these slots are matched to the ground-truth (GT) connections at minimum cost. One line thus becomes one chain of cells, and post-processing only cleans up overlapping lines. We also propose AI-CCQ, an asymmetric instance-level metric for thin, long lines. It gives credit to a partly drawn line but rejects a line that switches to a neighboring marking. On the SEED-MAP test set, STELLA reaches an AI-CCQ F1 score of 0.4483, against 0.3005 for a segmentation-based method and 0.3551 for the Sat2Graph representation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Domain shift remains a challenge for RGB-based surface-landmine detection, particularly when labeled imagery from a new environment is unavailable. This study examines unsupervised domain adaptation on SULAND_v2 using YOLOv8-S and a teacher–student framework. Complete acquisition sequences are separated between adaptation and evaluation, and seven adaptation configurations are compared across three source-training seeds. On the primary assignment, mean average precision at an intersection-over-union threshold of 0.50 (mAP50) increases from 53.70% for the source-only model to 63.56% for Mean Teacher and 64.22% for the CMT-style configuration. Controls with the same optimization budget indicate that additional training alone does not account for the Mean Teacher improvement. The pooled gain is concentrated in PMA-2, whereas PFM-1 performance decreases. Reversing the adaptation and evaluation sequences yields improvements for both classes, suggesting that the class response depends on the sequence assignment. Individual-sequence results and class-specific recall and false positives further reveal variation within the pooled scores. Additional alignment objectives provide small, inconsistent gains over Mean Teacher on the primary assignment, while source-domain accuracy decreases modestly. These findings suggest that unlabeled target imagery can improve detection within the evaluated dataset, although its benefits vary across classes and acquisitions. The intended application is candidate generation for visible surface targets and analyst review; operational reliability requires further validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of gross primary productivity (GPP) at fine spatial and temporal resolutions is essential for characterizing carbon uptake across heterogeneous landscapes. Coarse-resolution GPP products smooth differences among neighboring vegetation patches, while many fine-resolution products report only total GPP and do not distinguish the contributions of sunlit and shaded leaves. In this study, we developed SS-GPP30, a spatially continuous dataset that provides 8-day estimates of total GPP and its sunlit- and shaded-leaf components at 30 m resolution across the Beijing–Tianjin–Hebei (BTH) region from 2017 to 2025. The dataset was generated using a two-leaf light-use-efficiency framework driven by Harmonized Landsat and Sentinel-2 (HLS)-derived leaf area index, regional-scale ERA5 meteorological forcing (0.25°), annual 30 m land cover, and seasonal, vegetation-type-specific dynamic representative clumping index. The methodological advance over existing high-resolution GPP products lies in integrating 30 m HLS-based canopy dynamics with sunlit–shaded TL-LUE modeling and seasonal vegetation-type-specific dynamic clumping index, thereby enabling finer temporal representation of canopy photosynthesis and spatial characterization of heterogeneous landscapes. Evaluation against flux-derived GPP at eight eddy covariance sites showed that the seasonal index reduced the root-mean-square error in four of five vegetation and crop groups, with the largest reductions in forest (5.3%) and shrubland (4.6%). Cross-product comparisons across vegetation types yielded R2 values ranging from 0.70 to 0.89, while SS-GPP30 better preserved fine-scale spatial heterogeneity than coarser-resolution products. During 2017–2025, mean annual GPP was 1431.7 g C m−2 yr−1, with sunlit- and shaded-leaf GPP contributing 742.0 and 689.7 g C m−2 yr−1, respectively. Shaded leaves contributed 48.2% of total GPP and showed marked spatiotemporal variation. Both components increased significantly, with a stronger trend in shaded-leaf GPP. Therefore, SS-GPP30 fills an important gap in fine-resolution GPP mapping by providing 30 m estimates of total, sunlit-leaf, and shaded-leaf GPP, thereby resolving both regional vegetation productivity and within-canopy photosynthetic partitioning across the BTH region. The framework also has potential for application to other regions.

</details>
